## 2026-10-24 — MS1c SIGTERM-EPERM RED `5af8a36b`

**BLOCKED narrowly.** Exact `5af8a36b018800e2b425f2be594dfab2d2e0f4b2` adds only executable, unregistered `tests/mac/ms1c_term_eperm_red.py`, blob SHA-256 `6d05b82c413b1d9fece34e60a5eb21e1b9e41e9e8897c9958d30bf0c8540d76f`. It is absent from `build.zig`, passes `py_compile` and `git diff --check`, and was run from a clean archive under `env -i` with private HOME/TMP/XDG plus user/network/PID/mount namespaces. Current production exited 1 with 16 causal problems: EPERM escapes as a traceback; poll and actual pgid are not captured; a mismatched actual group is nevertheless targeted; the exec-arch socket diagnostic is masked; and no exact LEAK/failure identity reaches the direct or combined path. The synthetic real-gate boundary already passes: one exact leak makes the arch row nonzero, stops the host, retains the root and marker, and makes the next attempt HOST_HELD.

The submitted red is satisfiable. In scratch I changed only `stop_browser_term_only`: cache the pid and `poll()`, obtain the actual pgid, refuse a mismatch without signalling it, turn killpg EPERM into `_leak`, and preserve the existing wait/group behavior. The complete submitted oracle then printed its one PASS line. This establishes that the behavioral rows are coherent.

I can confirm a limited safety fact about the exact baseline: its fake `FakeProc` is not an operating-system process, exec-arch replaces `Popen`, and every browser signal the current production helper attempts goes through the test's replaced `teardown.os.killpg`; the gate row prints a transcript instead of launching a browser. Thus the reviewed baseline path made no real browser-signal call. I cannot certify the stronger invariant requested for the oracle and its future green/mutant passes. The driver does not replace `os.kill`, gives `FakeProc` no trapping `send_signal`, `terminate` or `kill` methods, and has no internal/private-PID-namespace capability guard. A green or mutant that switches to a direct pid signal could therefore execute a real host syscall, and a timeout could signal a test descendant outside a test-owned PID namespace.

The red also omits the fail-closed branch where `os.getpgid(pid)` itself raises `ProcessLookupError`, `PermissionError` or another `OSError`. That branch cannot establish the required actual group, so it must send nothing, emit one bounded exact identity-bearing LEAK/failure, and flow through retained/HOST_HELD handling. This matters because the existing teardown deliberately supports an already-exited/reaped leader with surviving group children; the fix must preserve registered T1–T9 behavior rather than blindly requiring `getpgid` after the leader is gone.

Fix-forward minimum, preserving every submitted row: replace both `os.killpg` and `os.kill` in each direct driver; add trapping `FakeProc.send_signal`, `.terminate` and `.kill`; record and reject every nonzero signal API other than the one fake `killpg(pid, SIGTERM)` EPERM attempt; and make the oracle self-reexec behind a causally verified private user/PID/network namespace (or an equivalently complete no-host-signal boundary) before any process or timeout is created. Add a `getpgid`-failure case requiring no signal, no traceback, exactly one bounded LEAK and failure, and the same retained/held gate outcome. A direct-kill/proc-kill mutant must be red without issuing a real signal.

After that red is approved, green must cache pid and `poll()` before signalling; for a live leader obtain and validate `os.getpgid(pid) == pid`; signal no mismatched or unconfirmed group; turn killpg EPERM into the existing exact identity-bearing leak path without waiting as if TERM was delivered; preserve the original exec-arch socket diagnostic, output absence, endpoint cleanup and caller directory; and retain the gate root/LEAKED marker with the next run HOST_HELD. Preserve the exited-leader/group-child contract in `mac_term_teardown_selftest.py` T1–T9. Register the new oracle directly under `test-mac-ms1c-gate` and aggregate `test`, with explicit inputs, side effects, private namespace execution and no skip mapping. Run EPERM-swallowed, mismatched-group-signalled, getpgid-failure-ignored, direct-kill/proc-kill, lost-diagnostic and gate-retention mutants through the registered step, then run both the full MS1c hostless gate and `test-mac-build-script`. No Mac contact or retry is authorized.

## R1–R4 review rules

### R1 — behavior proof versus source tripwires

Runtime behavior is accepted only from a driven test of the production seam or a bounded real-platform run. Source-text parsing, symbol checks, `grep`, `nm`, `strings`, `otool`, and similar checks are architecture tripwires only; they do not prove control flow or runtime behavior and cannot close a behavioral obligation.

### R2 — one exact minimum

The first `BLOCK` for a slice states the complete exact minimum needed to close it: named product changes, tests, registration, and platform evidence. Later reviews judge only that minimum. A later blocker may add an obligation only if new evidence establishes an R3-class defect; otherwise it is a non-blocking `SHOULD`.

### R3 — blocking threshold

`BLOCK` only for a product defect, a safety or security risk, a red registered gate, or a purported proof that cannot fail when the claimed behavior is broken. Missing evidence already named by the first minimum is a proof-that-cannot-fail blocker. Additional diagnostics, coverage, mutants, or oracle strengthening beyond that minimum are `SHOULD`s.

### R4 — lightweight compile-only Mac feedback

R4 is a separate compile/link-only evidence tier. An unchanged, reviewed harness digest may be used at one exact clean source commit on both matching hosts (x86_64 Intel and arm64 Apple Silicon), recording exact compiler/link arguments, results, artifact digests, and zero run-owned leftovers. It must not launch any newly built target, access credentials or live services, perform UI/signal/destructive work, install, use `sudo`, mutate persistent host state, or fetch dependencies. Any harness-byte change requires a new digest review. R4 proves only compilation/linking; it cannot satisfy runtime, containment, Keychain, UI, or owner-visible acceptance.

## Plan verification rule — prove it by running it

1. A behavior is proven only by running the real production function, C export, binary, or script with real inputs and asserting an observable result. A mirror or reimplementation proves nothing.
2. A test counts only when registered in a `build.zig` step reached by `zig build test` or the named platform gate. An unregistered test is a draft.
3. Proof requires red, green, and at least one mutation of production code that makes the test fail. The commit message records the mutant, command, and failing output. A mutant that stays green is not proof.
4. Platform code is proven only by a released run on the relevant platform. AppKit/Objective-C, Keychain, and Seatbelt require both Macs (x86_64 and arm64). Linux or cross-compilation proves only compilation.
5. Source and binary-inspection checks are tripwires, never behavioral proof.
6. The production entry point must reach the behavior. Unwired code is `NOT BUILT` even when an isolated unit test passes; an end-to-end test proves the production caller reaches it.
7. A skip, exit 77, or zero real rows/assertions under a gate is failure.
8. Validators and reviews use the same rule: `VERIFIED` or `passes` requires an observed run at a named commit with the command and exit code. Reading alone yields `SUSPECTED`.
9. Every behavior names its production entry point, test file and registered step, production mutant, and—where applicable—platform run and host(s). Missing any item means it is not implementation-ready.

## Mac run rule

No agent may contact either Mac by `ssh`/`scp` or execute a test binary there until both conditions occur in order:

1. SilverRidge approves the exact source commit and records the SHA-256 of **every** harness file the proposed run uses.
2. The orchestrator explicitly releases that exact reviewed run.

Approval never transfers across commits or changed harness bytes. The compile-only R4 tier is the default Mac evidence tier. Runtime platform proof requires its separately reviewed bounded harness and release. No Mac run may execute `tests/mac/ms6_5_reaper_gate.sh`, `hard_gate_driver.py`, or any `SIGKILL` path.

**APPROVE OPTION B; DO NOT USE OPTION A.** A Mac-only probe through a test-only entry point is the safer proof of the hook-16 guard. Option A would make the proof contingent on WebKit honoring `CFFIXED_USER_HOME`; if that assumption is false, merely discovering the failure performs the forbidden owner-Library write. The probe should inspect only the already-defined data-store-selection decision with `ZB_DEBUG_EPHEMERAL_DATA_STORE=1` and no `--e2e-protocol`; it must not instantiate either a persistent or non-persistent WebKit store. The production result must be the persistent-selection decision, the guard-drop mutant must select ephemeral, and the registered test must turn red under that mutant. Keep the entry point test-only and non-routable from normal application behavior so it does not become a production control surface. This choice does not relax the separate active two-launch isolation proof.

### `bb6a50b4bbe5c1d5c5fd81ca38c83b82bdc30f01` — complete two-Mac runtime-rows request

This establishes the runner's ordinary transport and verdict mechanics, not permission to launch WebKit.

The request has four independent blockers:

1. The owner has not ruled whether WebKit helper/framework writes under the account's Darwin per-user temp/cache area are exempt from the write-only-under-run-root rule. Per the orchestrator, no browser-launching Mac run may be approved while that answer is pending.

4. A TERM-ignoring browser produces a LEAK but the leg continues later rows and the unconditional EXIT trap deletes its run tree while it is still alive. That invalidates later observations on that host and can leave the survivor running after its private paths disappear. Before a live request, a leak must fail-stop that host's remaining rows, preserve and print the exact host/pgid/run directory for manual stop, and suppress automatic deletion for that host until the operator confirms the process is gone; add a hostless leak-row proof. No automated SIGKILL is permitted.

The accepted outcome per row on **each** architecture is:

- `mac_ephemeral_store_test.py`: rc 0 and exact `PASS\tms3-ephemeral-store`; each launch selects ephemeral once, launch 1 can set/read the cookie, launch 2 cannot see it, and there is no teardown leak.
- `mac_ms2a_load_fail_no_quit_test.py`: rc 1 and exact row-owned `RED_SHAPE`; rows 1–3 each have exactly one matching LOAD_FAIL, exit 5, and no page-list request; row 5 passes.
- `mac_ms2c_recover_slow_server_test.py`: rc 1 and exact shape; exit 8 at 13–19 seconds after phase 2 and health not asked.
- `mac_ms2c_close_button_test.py`: rc 1 and exact shape; popup still listed, routed title reply is `result`, live jobs return to baseline, opener stays intact, last count is `WINCOUNT\t2`.
- `mac_ms2b_dialog_popup_id_test.py`: rc 1 and exact shape; `window-2`, `open=False`, `error:no_window`, and confirm not true.
- `mac_cookie_timeout_test.py`: rc 1 and exact shape; getAll/set/delete each return `result` at 12.0–14.5 seconds, with live jobs back at baseline and browser alive.
- `mac_profile_pacing_persist_test.py`: at pre-green `91b9046`, rc 1 and exact historical shape (`missing`, at least one save error, `default-cap`); at `bb6a50b`, rc 0 and exact `PASS\tms3-profile-pacing-persist` (`present`, no save error, restored cap 7).

Risk rulings: **(1) BLOCK / OWNER DECISION REQUIRED.** Setting `TMPDIR` is worthwhile and already done, but it is not a proof for launchd/XPC WebKit helpers. `DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR` and `DARWIN_USER_CACHE_DIR` are `confstr`/`getconf` selector names, not supported per-process environment overrides; `CFFIXED_USER_HOME` affects CoreFoundation home resolution only in processes that receive it. `launchctl setenv` would be session-global, race with other work and pooled helpers, and is not an acceptable per-run confinement proof. There is no reviewed harness-only environment setting that guarantees all helper/framework writes stay below the run root. If the owner keeps the literal rule, use a dedicated disposable account/VM/session plus an owner-authorized filesystem-write audit, or add a stronger OS-enforced confinement design; do not infer zero disk writes from `nonPersistentDataStore`.

**(2) BLOCK IN THE CURRENT RUNNER SHAPE, NOT A LICENSE TO ESCALATE.** Leaving the process alive is the correct consequence of the no-SIGKILL rule and the row correctly fails, but subsequent rows and deletion of the live process's private root are not acceptable. Manual stop must occur against the reported exact identity before cleanup or further accepted work on that host.

**(3) ACCEPTED.** Red values are hypotheses to establish on the Macs. The runner correctly treats any different rc/value/timing/precondition as `MISMATCH`, exits nonzero, and never upgrades it to a pass. ## 2026-10-22 — cf16730 runtime-row/profile fix-forwards

### `0e0a58e` — recording functions still do not bind the real observation call sites

**BLOCK; THE `cf16730` EXACT-VALUE MINIMUM IS STILL OPEN.** The new row-owned recording functions are a sound improvement, and the registered selftest now drives those functions with accepted and same-polarity counter observations. It retains S1–S9, D1, the runner mismatch rows, and all value predicates; the exact unmodified selftest passes 189 cases. Pacing also correctly tightens the historical save failure to `FileNotFound` on the exact pacing file.

The remaining gap is precisely at the boundary the minimum required. The P check only counts each recording-function call in `main` and scans for direct `shape.check` calls; it does not verify that `main` passes the raw runtime observation. In an exact archive of `0e0a58ee9b5298d58ec8bbc22f98bf14ddcbe886`, replacing the real close reply call
`record_routed(shape, rpc.raw("page.title", {"window_id": popup_id}))` with the accepted constant `record_routed(shape, {"result": {"title": "POPUP"}})` left the registered oracle green: `PASS mac-red-shape-selftest 6 red tests, 189 cases`. The function is still called exactly once and its independent D cases still pass, while the Mac row can no longer observe the browser's reply. Equivalent accepted constants can still be supplied at the recovery, count, dialog, cookie, and pacing call sites.

Narrow fix-forward minimum: drive the acquisition-and-recording boundary itself by injecting the process/RPC/output source into row-owned functions, or add an equally behavioral proof of the real call arguments. Every accepted-constant substitution at the actual `main` call site must make the registered step red; a call-count/source tripwire alone is insufficient. No Mac run using these predicted red values is eligible yet.

### `f973d05` — leak fail-stop is good, but timeout does not create the later-run hold

**BLOCK AGAINST `cf16730` ITEM 2.** The ordinary leak path is correctly fail-stop and SIGTERM-only: a row's `LEAK` line causes no later row to run, exact pid/pgid/start/command records are written under the run root, the host and retained root are printed, that root is removed from automatic cleanup, and the other architecture may finish. A later leg sees an existing `../zb-gate-*/LEAKED`, runs zero rows, and exits held. The private-PID-namespace teardown proof now validates the real identity line, and the exact ancestry's registered `test-mac-build-script` passed 9/9 steps including the expanded callers proof.

The timeout branch does not actually receive the same durable hold. Only the remote row-leak branch writes `$root/LEAKED` (lines 106–110 at the exact commit). Outer exit 124 merely drops the current directory from cleanup and prints `STOPPED`; it writes no marker. The next run checks only `../zb-gate-*/LEAKED`, so it can run rows on that host despite the prior timeout and possible survivor. The hostless L control asserts zero deletion for rc 124 but never follows that timeout with a second leg; V8 covers only an already present leak marker.

Narrow fix-forward minimum: establish a durable in-run marker before starting the remote leg and remove it only after a clean completion, or otherwise guarantee that exit 124 leaves a marker the next run checks. Add a registered two-run timeout control requiring the first run to keep the root and the second to make zero row/process launches and report the exact host/run root as held. Preserve the current leak identity proof and the no-SIGKILL rule.

### `c4b2376` — outer profile delegation is driven, but Linux OOM semantics regress

**BLOCK UNTIL THE LINUX DEFAULT-PROFILE ALLOCATION FAILURE REMAINS FAIL-CLOSED.** The requested outer-delegation proof itself is now satisfied. `app.applyProfileDir` is one call into `profile_step.apply`, all macOS/Windows/Linux branches live behind the injected binder, and the registered test behaviorally drives all three. From an exact clean archive, `zig build -j4 test-app-profile-dir-macos cross-check --summary all` passed all 5 profile tests and 26/26 steps. The macOS test still proves directory-before-bind and real pacing-file persistence; Windows explicit/null/refusal and Linux explicit/default/clear/refusal paths are exercised. This is the right abstraction and the relative-socket fix remains intact.

The disclosed Linux change is not acceptable as incidental refactor behavior. Previously, failure of the second `arena.dupeZ` that prepares the resolved default profile for `zb_app_set_profile_dir` returned `error.WriteFailed`. `ShimProfileBinder.bind` now collapses that allocation failure into `false`, and `profile_step.apply(.linux)` maps any false bind of a default profile to `null`. Under memory pressure the browser can therefore continue without the selected persistent profile, losing persistence and falling back to WebKit's default context, instead of failing closed. That is both user-visible and capable of redirecting state writes; it is outside the 4pa.60 fix.

Preserve the old distinction, for example by making `bind` return an error union so allocation failure maps to `WriteFailed` while a genuine shim refusal of the optional default can retain its existing null fallback. Add an injected allocation/error test for the Linux default path. Until that lands, `c4b23765873c08abcdcb34479aecc02609ed60bc` is not safe to push as the closure. Independently, 4pa.60 still needs its pre-green Mac red and post-green Mac pass under the exact-commit/harness-digest/orchestrator-release rule.

Because `0e0a58e` and `f973d05` are ancestors of `4a68e34`/`c4b2376`, and `c4b2376` adds the OOM regression, the current ancestry tip is **not push-safe as a reviewed whole**, even though the 4pa.62 parser and 3ui.9 part-1 slices above are independently approved. The owner `/var/folders` decision continues to hold every browser-launching Mac run. No Mac was contacted or authorized.

The oracle drives the real `run` control flow through executing SSH/SCP stubs in a private user/network namespace. Its stager stand-in is appropriately limited to recording the production arguments and materializing the documented bundle shape; the eventual released run, not that stand-in, is the R1 proof of the real stager and Apple verifier.

The `run` half of `2e24fe98f44034ee34760c3939fc6f57be417729` is otherwise a sound experiment. It refuses a missing arm host, unknown mutant, and mismatching input manifest before transport; uses `mac_build` and the reviewed pinned/no-display/no-askpass transport; binds the build and committed agent tree to one commit; stages beneath the fresh run directory; signs each Mach-O deepest-first, the real framework version rather than `Current`, then the app, never using `--deep` to sign; and runs strict deep verification last. It records the data census, sign-failure count, verifier rc, and both named diagnostics, continues to the second architecture after a failed first leg, and removes both run directories at exit. No browser is launched or installed.

Registration of the run-phase oracle is additive (`build.zig` +8/-0) and real. From an exact green archive, `zig build -j4 test-mac-bundle-layout-spike test-mac-build-script --summary all` exited 0 with 12/12 steps. All eight independently recreated production mutants were red: S1 app signing with `--deep` (16 failures), S2 app-first signing (2), S3 verification without `--strict` (2), S4 ignoring verifier rc (1), S5 ignoring mutant mode (2), S6 counting Mach-O as data (4), S7 omitting the input-manifest check (1), and S8 signing the `Current` symlink (8). Both commit diffs pass `git diff --check`, shell syntax passes, and every submitted SHA-256 for the twelve repository files plus the Python package pin matches the exact green.

The blocker is the undriven and unpinned **`prep`** path. It runs `pip -q install sigstore`, allowing the current package and dependency set from PyPI, despite the authoritative plan requiring committed `packaging/macos/sigstore_verifier.txt` with `--require-hashes --no-deps`. The Python pkg bytes themselves are checked against the rev-15 SHA and the downloaded bundle is checked for `thomas@python.org` / Google identity, but that does not make the verifier executable supply chain pinned. The script also duplicates URL, SHA and identity values instead of consuming the committed `agent_runtime_pins.json`, and it does not validate that file's recorded `uv.lock` and verifier-requirements hashes. Calling this “exactly as `ms5_deps_gate.sh`” reproduces that gate's already-recorded V46-F10 defect rather than satisfying it.

This is also a proof-that-cannot-fail gap under R1/R3: the registered selftest never calls `prep`. In an exact-green scratch tree, deleting the package SHA check and the entire Sigstore verification from production `prep` still left `test-mac-bundle-layout-spike` green, 3/3, with every B row passing. Separately, the proposed plain Linux command does not write only to its output/scratch tree as claimed: with no cache environment, `uv cache dir` resolves to `/home/maceo/.cache/uv` and pip's cache to `/home/maceo/.cache/pip`.

Narrow fix-forward minimum: make `prep` read the package URL/SHA/identity/issuer, `uv.lock` SHA, requirements path and requirements SHA from the exact commit's `agent_runtime_pins.json`; validate both committed-file hashes; install the verifier with `pip install --require-hashes --no-deps -r <committed requirements>`; and confine HOME/XDG/uv/pip caches to the prep scratch tree (or disable those caches). Extend the registered hostless oracle to execute the real `prep` entry with recording network/tool stubs and prove the exact verifier argv, package verification argv, pinned-file/hash use, and cache paths. Removing the pkg hash or identity call, restoring bare `pip install sigstore`, corrupting/hash-stripping the requirements file, or redirecting a cache outside scratch must make the registered step red before any Mac contact.

The proposed Mac run is **NOT APPROVED**, so no exact commit or digest set is released. The submitted digest list is accurate for `2e24fe9` but is not an approval manifest; a fix-forward request needs a new exact commit and fresh hashes, including `agent_runtime_pins.json` and `sigstore_verifier.txt`. Its accepted observations must require both normal legs to reach staging with zero sign failures and report strict-verifier results; a clean rc 0 means the current layout survives, while a verifier-only failure is the layout finding. A staging/sign/transport failure is inconclusive. The `data_in_frameworks` run must reach both strict verifiers with zero sign failures and fail both legs; a pass or incomplete leg is inconclusive.

The no-browser scope avoids the WebKit-helper-specific portion of the `/var/folders` concern, but **does not exempt this request from the underlying zero-exception rule that Mac writes stay in the fresh run directory**. The request itself acknowledges that `/usr/bin/python3`/xcrun and `codesign` may write per-user Darwin cache data outside that directory; `TMPDIR` does not prove otherwise, and there is no reviewed per-process `DARWIN_USER_CACHE_DIR` override. Therefore the owner's `/var/folders` decision applies to this run too: a re-request still needs an explicit owner exception for these toolchain/cache writes or a reviewed containment/write-audit proof. The older `0e0a58e`, `f973d05`, and `c4b2376` defects do not execute in this non-browser spike and would not independently invalidate its observation, but they keep the ancestry tip not push-safe as a whole. No Mac was contacted.

Non-blocking hygiene: remove the selftest's unused `rd`, `files_done`, and `bundle_seen` locals when touching it next.

A compiler-only unused-parameter failure is not that proof. Preserve the currently passing green row, M1 behavior, and compiling timeout-drop behavior.

`zigBrowser-3ui.11` may close as a **gate fix** after that fix-forward. The shipped-product suspicion should be split into a separate product bead before closure rather than silently discarded: the build-only mitigation does not change the browser, Linux was sampled only five times, Windows' GlobalBlock is not exposed, and macOS' PosixBlock remains unverified. ## 2026-10-23 — 4pa.55 macOS failed-load policy

### `1ef8301` red + `ca4ab28` green

**BLOCK GREEN; `1ef8301170ee9a2c1ed4eb9dd48fae1cdc651a34` IS SAFE TO PUSH ONLY AS AN UNREGISTERED SCAFFOLDING RED, BUT IT IS NOT THE REQUESTED BEHAVIORAL RED, AND `ca4ab288eba0746e56fd3bf66347418663dca12a` IS NOT SAFE TO PUSH AS THE 4PA.55 CLOSURE.** The new portable policy itself is correct: its exhaustive state property permits process exit and the 15-second exit timer only for an untagged, non-recovery load in the primary window before that window's first finished load. Routed failures report to their caller; later primary failures, recovery failures, and every popup/policy-window failure record without exiting. The C enum values are tied to the real header, `main.zig` retains the two exports, and the normal invalid-URL and both WKNavigation failure callbacks call the policy rather than retaining the old unconditional failure exit.

Registration is real and the green portable proof passes. From an exact clean archive with private HOME/XDG/Zig caches and display/askpass variables unset, `zig build -j4 test-load-failure-policy cross-check --summary all` exited 0 with 26/26 steps and all seven policy tests. Cross-check compiled the complete shim/object graph for both x86_64-macos and aarch64-macos. A production mutant returning `.exit` for every untagged failure made five named tests fail; an independent mutant arming every timer made four named tests fail. Thus the pure Zig decision is non-vacuous.

The blocking product defect is a second timer policy in Objective-C. At the exact green, `-[ZBWindowHost reloadBypassingCache:]` still unconditionally schedules `ZB_NAVIGATION_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, and `onNavigationTimeout:` still unconditionally emits `LOAD_FAIL` and calls `requestExit:ZB_EXIT_TIMEOUT`. Neither consults `zb_load_exit_timer_armed` or `zb_load_failure_action`. The new decision is used only by `loadURI:`; therefore a later primary reload reached through the exported `zb_reload` path can still end the whole browser after 15 seconds, contrary to the commit's and request's “timer armed only then” claim. This is an actual second copy of the policy, not just missing test coverage.

The current hostless oracle does not bind even the one edited shim call site. Independently replacing `loadURI:`'s `zb_load_exit_timer_armed(...)` guard with `YES` left both `test-load-failure-policy` and `cross-check` green: 26/26 steps and 7/7 tests. Cross compilation proves that symbols and Objective-C syntax link; it does not prove which load initiators use the decision.

The exact red also does not fail at its named rows on parent behavior. Running its test at exact `1ef8301` stops before any test with `src/load_failure_policy.zig ... FileNotFound`; the matching header constants are absent there too. The two recreated production-semantic mutants above demonstrate that the eventual oracle rejects the old quit-all/arm-all behavior at named rows, but that is mutant evidence from the green, not an executable behavioral red at the submitted red SHA.

Narrow fix-forward minimum:

1. Remove the unconditional timer arm from `reloadBypassingCache:`, or centralize every browser load/reload timer through one helper that calls `zb_load_exit_timer_armed` with the correct primary/finished/recovery state. A timer expiry outside the eligible initial load must record/continue rather than call `requestExit`.
2. Extend the registered hostless proof to bind the real shim timer call sites: enumerate every browser-host `ZB_NAVIGATION_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` scheduling path (excluding the separate screenshot runner), require each to flow through the Zig decision, and make both a `loadURI:` guard-drop and a `reloadBypassingCache:` unconditional-arm mutant red. Compile-only success is not that proof.
3. Supply a committed behavioral old-semantics control—such as a registered mutant selftest for quit-all plus arm-all—whose failures name the policy rows, so the plan has an executable red rather than only a missing-file compile stop. Preserve the two direct Zig mutants above.

The rewrite of `mac_ms2a_user_nav_failure_test.py` is **approved and not weakened**. The old oracle checked only that the browser exited within eight seconds—precisely the behavior 4pa.55 removes. The new oracle first requires routed navigation to succeed, then requires exactly one matching `LOAD_FAIL` for the untagged dead-host navigation, requires the browser to remain alive after the failure grace, and requires `page.list` still to answer. The old implementation emits its one row and immediately exits with load-failed, so it fails the new explicit exit assertion; the older per-window suppression defect yields zero matching rows. Private HOME/profile and the e2e-gated ephemeral store strengthen isolation. The test-tamper alert is therefore an intentional specification correction, not assertion deletion.

Mac runtime remains **UNPROVEN and HELD** on the owner's `/var/folders` decision. After the hostless fix-forward, closure still requires the separately reviewed exact-commit/two-Mac run with every harness SHA-256 and orchestrator release. No Mac was contacted or authorized.

### `a97607a` Linux-scope fix-forward

**APPROVED; `a97607ab277eadc1b05ba4b6dd538657f71b6298` SATISFIES THE `99a44f3` MINIMUM, IS SAFE TO PUSH FOR THIS SCOPE, AND `zigBrowser-3ui.11` MAY CLOSE AS A GATE FIX.** `runInProcessGtkTests` now returns the exact plain `b.addRunArtifact(tests)` for every target other than native Linux. Only the native-Linux branch installs the GNU `timeout` command, creates an owned run-step environment, and removes `DESKTOP_STARTUP_ID`, `GIO_LAUNCHED_DESKTOP_FILE`, `GIO_LAUNCHED_DESKTOP_FILE_PID`, and `XDG_ACTIVATION_TOKEN`. All three independently recreated fix-forward controls were red at the owning evidence. M1, replacing the native-Linux removal loop with `_ = gtk_inherited_env`, made Linux report `env=unchanged stripped=0`; with the four variables inherited, the deliberate failure then hung through the 300-second outer bound and lost its test-name diagnostic, while the separately timed hang probe still ended normally. M2, replacing the timeout setup with the compiling `_ = timeout_s`, made Linux report `wrapped=0`; the ordinary failure remained an rc-1 named failure, but the hang probe exceeded 300 seconds. These are behavioral failures, not unused-variable/compiler tripwires.

The fix-forward changes only `build.zig` and `tests/e2e/gtk_test_env_selftest.py`; it removes no file/directory input from another step and introduces no product change. The registered oracle remains attached to aggregate `test`. The separately filed `zigBrowser-3ui.12` preserves the unresolved shipped-product question (including macOS verification), so it no longer blocks closure of this gate-only issue. Any future Mac execution remains governed by R1–R4, the exact-commit and complete harness-SHA-256 rule, orchestrator release, and all standing Mac holds. No Mac was contacted or authorized.

## 2026-10-23 — 4pa.80 Mac write-containment design (Agent Mail 8387)

**APPROVED TO IMPLEMENT WITH THE CHANGES BELOW. THIS IS A DESIGN APPROVAL, NOT A MAC-RUN APPROVAL.** Kernel-enforced Seatbelt confinement is an acceptable replacement for an owner exception for this no-browser spike. Environment variables cannot override `confstr` and a timestamp audit cannot prove attribution, whereas the existing `mac_compile_only.sh` form—default access, deny every file write, then admit only the exact physical run root and `/dev/null`—fails a directly writing tool closed. The audit is useful defense in depth but is not the enforcement boundary.

Required implementation boundary:

1. Confinement must cover **every write-capable toolchain phase on the Mac**, not only staging, signing, and verification. In particular the native `zig build` reached through `mac_build`/`build-on-mac.sh`, and all of its xcrun/compiler/linker children, must run beneath the reviewed profile too. The post-build shell must likewise keep unpacking, `stage_agent_runtime.sh`, its absolute `/usr/bin/python3`/xcrun path, `pkgutil`, `ditto`, relocation-time codesign, final signing, and verification beneath the profile. Resolving a “real” Python or calling `xcrun --no-cache` outside that boundary would reopen the defect. If the absolute Python shim works inside the sandbox, it may remain; if it receives EPERM, that is a finding, not permission to resolve it outside.
2. Operations outside Seatbelt are limited to the unavoidable, structurally bound envelope: create one validated fresh physical run-root entry, transfer committed inputs only into that root, perform read-only pre/post audits, and remove that exact root. The root must match the controlled gate name, resolve with `pwd -P` to itself, and not be a symlink. The implementation must reject any staged symlink that resolves outside the root (internal framework symlinks remain valid). No inherited writable regular-file descriptor may point outside the root. The generated profile must contain no write grant for `$HOME`, `/var/folders`, `/private/tmp`, cache directories, or a parent of the run root.
3. Each leg must start with the **real `/usr/bin/sandbox-exec`** parsing that exact profile, a positive write below the run root, a positive `/dev/null` write, and unique pre-absent negative attempts for the real home and both `getconf` Darwin temp/cache directories. The negative attempts and the real work must use the same profile and wrapper. A missing `sandbox-exec`, profile-parse error, failed positive, or denied real tool is safe **INCONCLUSIVE / NOT RUN** and executes no later product row. An outside canary that is not refused is instead a **CONTAINMENT BREACH**, because it has already written outside the root; it must stop all later rows on that host, report and retain the exact target/evidence for owner handling, and must never be described as safe or automatically cleaned away.
4. A sandbox-denied real-tool failure cannot count as the spike's expected product evidence. The normal leg requires every build/stage/sign/verify phase to succeed. The `data_in_frameworks` leg must reach signing with zero stage/sign failures and fail verification with the accepted code-signature diagnostic (for example the expected unsealed-content result); a generic EPERM, missing output, timeout, or other phase failure is inconclusive. Logs and phase verdicts must be copied into the Linux-side evidence before the run root is removed.
5. Keep the read-only audit, but snapshot before and after rather than relying only on `find -newer <marker>`: marker time granularity misses cases and cannot report deletions. At minimum record path plus stable metadata for both `getconf` roots, `$HOME/Library/Caches`, and the intended shallow `$HOME` surface, excluding only the exact run-root lifecycle. Any unexplained delta makes the leg inconclusive. Noise on the personal arm64 host is a false-negative cost, not grounds to waive the boundary.
6. The registered hostless proof must drive the production wrappers, not only compare prose/profile text. It must prove the exact two sandboxed outer commands (native build and post-build work), the exact physical run-root substitution, canary ordering before real work, phase classification, and fail-closed cleanup/evidence behavior. In addition to the proposed mutants, require an unwrapped native build, unwrapped Python/xcrun resolution, unwrapped codesign/verify, an outside-root symlink, and a sandbox denial masquerading as the accepted `data_in_frameworks` failure to turn the registered step red. Retain the proposed `$HOME` grant, dropped-canary, unwrapped-stager, and non-refused-canary mutants.

Answers to the design questions: **(a)** yes, Seatbelt enforcement plus live confstr/home canaries and the secondary audit is acceptable with the whole-leg and outcome changes above; **(b)** yes, a deny-caused real-tool failure is safe but inconclusive, while a non-refused outside canary is a breach; **(c)** implement the hostless red/green now—there is no reason to wait for the owner merely to write and review non-contacting code.

After a hostless implementation satisfies this minimum at an exact reviewed commit, I may approve a 4pa.80 two-Mac request **without the owner's `/var/folders` exception**, because the released run will begin with the live kernel/profile canaries and will stop before substantive work on every safe failure. That is not automatic approval: the request still needs the exact commit, SHA-256 of the profile generator, wrappers, selftest, stager, build driver and every executed harness/input, accepted per-leg outcome vectors, reviewer approval, and orchestrator release. This design does not lift the global hold for browser-launching rows—WebKit/XPC helpers are a different boundary—or for any other Mac harness not proven to put its complete Mac-side execution under the same enforcement. No Mac was contacted or authorized.

**BLOCK AS THE `de82cf2` RED; `acfad8fea54f2f1ebf518248206292bf53d334e7` IS ONLY SAFE AS UNREGISTERED, INCOMPLETE SCAFFOLDING AND IS NOT THE ACCEPTED CONTAINMENT CONTRACT.** The test is genuinely red and its existing rows are useful. The causal vector is nevertheless clear: today's spike emits no `LEG` verdicts, invokes no Seatbelt wrapper, runs zig/xcrun/Python/staging/file/codesign outside confinement, uses the symlink spelling rather than the physical root, attempts no canaries, exports no evidence, does not classify the injected failure modes, and has no leg driver for K13. The no-contact boundary is sound. Before constructing the scratch repository the oracle re-execs itself in a private user/mount/network namespace. It overlays `/usr/bin` only in that private mount namespace, uses only fake hosts `intel@10.0.0.1` and `arm@10.0.0.2`, places executing ssh/scp stubs first on PATH, rejects every other host, and gives the production runner a nonexistent key. K1–K13 cover much of `de82cf2`: K1 binds the exact profile, physical-root spelling, two outer sandbox commands, ordering, and instrumented children; K2/K5/K6 bind the canaries and breach versus safe-inconclusive classes; K7/K8/K11 distinguish expected layout evidence from sandbox denial and generic failure; K9 rejects an escaping staged symlink; K4 checks copied evidence and cleanup; and K12 refuses bad local inputs before transport. Nothing drives the Darwin temp root, Darwin cache root, shallow HOME surface, a deletion, or a metadata-only change, although `de82cf2` required all four surfaces and specifically replaced `find -newer` because it misses deletions.
3. **The fail-before-product predicates omit prohibited tools.** `product_calls()` counts only zig, stager, and codesign. K5 and K6 can therefore remain green after an unwrapped xcrun, `/usr/bin/python3`, or `file` call, even though `de82cf2` expressly forbids resolving Python/xcrun before confinement and requires no later product phase after a failed preflight or canary.

Narrow fix-forward minimum:

1. Record and structurally classify **every** ssh/scp call made by the real runner. Admit only the exact root-create/validation, exact committed or hash-pinned input transfers into that root, read-only audit/evidence transport, run, and exact-root cleanup envelope; make any extra remote command, extra local input, outside-root remote path, or unknown call red. Add a row where the controlled run-root name is a symlink to an outside directory and require refusal before any sandbox or product tool, leaving the target unchanged.
2. Drive the inherited-descriptor rule for fd 1, fd 2, and an extra writable descriptor such as fd 9 pointing outside the root, with pipe and root-local positive controls. Every outside regular descriptor must stop before the first sandbox/product call.
3. Extend the audit proof to both architectures and all required surfaces: the real `getconf` temp root, `getconf` cache root, `$HOME/Library/Caches`, and shallow HOME. Require path plus stable metadata in both snapshots; drive additions/changes across the surfaces and at least one deletion, each producing a named INCONCLUSIVE verdict on the affected leg. A mutant dropping any surface or reverting to `find -newer` must be red.
4. For K5/K6, require the complete tool log to be empty—not only zig/stager/codesign—and assert both leg verdicts/details where both legs are expected. Preserve all current K1–K13 controls and, with the eventual green, demonstrate every `de82cf2` wrapper/profile/canary/symlink/denial mutant against the registered step.

This block does not authorize a Mac run. A later green still needs review at its exact commit before the 4pa.80 spike can request the Seatbelt-based exception to the owner hold.

**THE RELEASED STAGE A AT `154bf5a35ef5bfb743bed346838604d48d2ce3ab` FAILED SAFELY AND IS NOT EVIDENCE. STAGE B REMAINS HELD. Passing the internal pkg hash check before the failure does not preserve or independently identify the deleted pkg, so it is correctly not counted as a confirmed Stage A output.

The cause is causal and independently reproduced offline. On this host `/usr/bin/python3 -m venv <scratch>` exits 1 because Debian's Python 3.13.5 lacks `ensurepip`; the production script suppresses that specific diagnostic and reports only the combined verifier-install failure. In a private user/network namespace with private HOME, TMP, XDG, uv-cache, and uv-Python roots, the pinned `/home/maceo/.local/bin/uv` successfully ran `uv venv --offline --python /usr/bin/python3 <scratch>` and the resulting interpreter ran as CPython 3.13.5. Installing a system package is neither needed nor approved.

The observed `2a04:4e42:5000::223` connection is **INCONCLUSIVE NETWORK ATTRIBUTION, NOT A PROVEN CLOSED-HOST BREACH**. The 5894aef0 rule authorized hostnames, not a frozen DNS-address list; a CDN may return different IPv4/IPv6 addresses for successive connections. Conversely, the passive `ss` sampler cannot prove the TLS hostname or effective URL, and the exact production curl uses `-L`, so the Fastly operator assertion is not proof that no cross-host redirect occurred. The sampler was useful diagnosis but is not accepted release evidence. No incident is inferred from the address alone; the next harness must make curl itself report and enforce the boundary.

**APPROVED FIX DIRECTION: UV VENV; NO SYSTEM PACKAGE INSTALL. Narrow red/green minimum:**

1. Commit an unregistered causal red that reaches the real prep environment without network and demonstrates the current system-Python `venv` failure as its named row; do not satisfy it with the present Python stub that fabricates `bin/pip` and `bin/python`. Use `--no-config` and an explicit `https://pypi.org/simple` default index for every networked uv verifier/site install so ambient uv configuration cannot change the authorized source. Preserve the private HOME/XDG/UV/PIP roots and the existing pin, lock, binary-only, no-dependency, and hash checks.
3. The registered offline proof must execute the **real uv**, not a venv-creating stub, inside a private network namespace: create the environment from `/usr/bin/python3`, run its interpreter, and use real `uv pip install` against a local, hash-locked stand-in wheel (`--offline --no-index --find-links`) followed by an import from that environment. A production mutant restoring `python3 -m venv`, dropping the explicit target interpreter, installing outside the created environment, or dropping `--require-hashes` must turn the registered step red.
4. Harden both curl downloads so redirects are never accepted and the evidence comes from curl, not an IP poll. Each transfer must record its requested URL, `url_effective`, `remote_ip`, and HTTP status in captured Stage A output; require status 200 and exact effective-URL equality with the pinned pkg URL or its exact `.sigstore` derivative. ## 2026-10-23 — 4pa.22 pre-red backend-Protocol design ruling

**APPROVED TO WRITE THE RED WITH `state_dirs=None` AS AN EXPLICIT BACKEND-DEFAULT SENTINEL.** Plan R29-MS6 I1 is internally inconsistent as written: it requires identical Protocol/backend defaults, but the shared Protocol currently defaults `state_dirs` to Linux's nonempty `STATE_DIRS` while also requiring macOS to reject every nonempty ignored `state_dirs` input. Copying that value into the Mac signature would make an ordinary default Mac launch reject itself. This ruling is the implementation minimum for the red; it does not pre-approve a green or count the signature oracle as production proof.

The Protocol and both implementations must expose the identical `state_dirs=None` default. Linux must normalize only `None` to its existing `STATE_DIRS`; macOS must normalize only `None` to `()`. An explicit `()` remains a real caller choice meaning no state directories on either backend, and an explicit custom Linux tuple retains its current behavior. The public Linux `helper_argv(..., state_dirs=STATE_DIRS)` behavior need not and must not change. The red must freeze the current Linux default command/mount result, prove that `None` reproduces it, and separately prove that explicit empty omits those state directories; this prevents a green from silently changing Linux confinement while making the signatures match.

The same red may cover the plan's other conformance corrections: `launch_spec(..., network=None)` on the Protocol and both implementations, `network(session=None)`, and `egress_dir() -> Optional[str]`. `network=` needs a behavioral sentinel that reaches the returned `LaunchSpec.network` and its rendered endpoint/bridge behavior; signature introspection alone is a tripwire. Keep default normalization in one named statement and use flat fail-fast guards before any profile materialization or other side effect.

For the present BROAD macOS state, accept `state_dirs=None`, explicit `state_dirs=()`, and `ro_paths`; the existing broad `allow file-read*` really does make every requested read available, so rejecting `ro_paths` would misdescribe capability rather than improve safety. This is temporary compatibility, not read-curation proof: the red should name it as a BROAD-state tripwire, MS6.7 must later bind requested reads in the curated profile, and MS6.8 remains the required driven `launch.build_launch` proof. macOS must raise `UnsupportedLaunchInput` before creating a profile for each independently driven unsupported value: `seccomp_fd is not None`, `net_dir is not None`, `claude_writable is True`, and an explicit nonempty `state_dirs`. Default and explicit-empty state dirs must be separate rows so `None` cannot be conflated with `()`.

The registered red must therefore cover the full signature/default matrix; frozen Linux default, explicit-empty and custom state-dir behavior; Mac default/empty success plus each one-field refusal and no leaked profile artifact; current BROAD `ro_paths` acceptance; the behavioral network sentinel; and the optional egress result. At minimum, mutants that resolve Linux `None` to empty, conflate Mac `None` with explicit empty, ignore each unsupported nondefault, drop `network`, or change a parameter/default must turn the registered step red. The test must be in aggregate `test` with no skip-to-green mapping. 4pa.22 remains open after this tripwire lands: the real conformance claim still waits for MS6.8's driven production boundary.

## 2026-10-23 — 4pa.77 / 3ui.9 parts 2–3 direct-transport green

### `75b1e882`

**BLOCK.** `75b1e8822a4b312c4b2d80b4e2d83aeb53cff207` closes the original H1–H7 transport rows in a clean checkout, but three exact-commit launchers still take the trust root from the mutable outer working tree. The disclosed commit itself changes exactly the stated 19 files, has no mode change, and its `build.zig` hunk is additive. All 18 changed shell files parse. From an exact clean archive under private HOME/TMP and a private user/network namespace, `mac_direct_transport_hostkey_selftest.sh` passed all 18 rows: the 16 driven scripts made non-vacuous calls, the destructive reaper gate was only definition-evaluated, and H7's census was complete. The exact registered command `zig build test-mac-build-script test-mac-compile-only test-mac-reaper-gate-guard -j2` exited 0 with 16/16 steps; the new direct-transport check, both reaper refusal checks, compile-only, shared transport, direct-cache, caller, log-integrity, and setup-hostkey checks all passed. The green therefore materially improves every direct line: `StrictHostKeyChecking=yes`, one pinned file, batch/identity-only operation, and all display/askpass variables removed. `ms6_gate.sh` no longer trusts `/dev/null` and does set `SSH_ASKPASS_REQUIRE=never`.

The remaining defect is concrete, not a request for broader polish. `mac_compile_only.sh` authenticates its executing bytes through `ZB_COMPILE_HARNESS_SHA256` and exports the requested source commit, but assigns `KNOWN_HOSTS="$TOP/tests/mac/known_hosts"` before that export. `ms6_hard_gate.sh` and `ms7_appkit_gate.sh` require `BASH_EXECUTION_STRING` to equal `git show <sha>:<launcher>`, then export that commit and claim to read only the export, but both likewise assign `KNOWN_HOSTS="$PWD/tests/mac/known_hosts"` before entering it. A dirty outer `known_hosts` can therefore change the accepted host key while the approved launcher and source commit remain identical. The registered H2 oracle blesses precisely that outer scratch-worktree path, so it cannot detect this substitution. The commit's actual `mac_compile_only.sh` SHA-256 is `58e1ae7147907987aa1351007b5f7103ad334ca48c4616ee0fa8251cf634c1ca`, not the `d06fb8ed6…` prefix stated in the submission; any later compile-only request must bind the full independently recomputed digest.

Exact fix-forward minimum:

1. For `mac_compile_only.sh`, `ms6_hard_gate.sh`, and `ms7_appkit_gate.sh`, make `UserKnownHostsFile` name bytes derived from the same exact approved commit as the executing/exported harness—prefer the corresponding file inside the guarded exact-commit export, or write `git show "$SHA:tests/mac/known_hosts"` into the guarded scratch tree and verify it against that blob. A mutant that restores the outer-worktree path must turn the registered step red for each launcher mode.
3. Preserve the current H1–H7 rows, the destructive reaper non-execution boundary, `has_side_effects = true`, and the existing M1–M8 results; rerun the exact registered commands above. The helper side of the green is acceptable for this spike. It classifies code by Mach-O magic rather than name or execute bit; exempts only the validated `agent-host/Python.framework` shape; relocates only agent-host data; preserves the logical namespace, bytes, modes, intentional links and consumer paths; refuses the named destination/collision/link/special-file cases before mutation; journals and rolls back injected failures; is deterministic and second-run-refusing; and leaves non-agent-host Frameworks data to the census, where it makes the candidate inconclusive. The leg checks escaping symlinks both before and after relocation. Candidate-before-control gating is exact-commit bound, records `STARTED` before first host contact, refuses a repeated mode, and permits the control only after both candidate legs are `LAYOUT-OK` at the same commit. The blocker is concrete. Candidate classification at `bundle_layout_spike.sh:244-247` accepts any positive number of `SIGNFAIL` rows when field 3 is `not-signed` and field 4 is merely nonempty. Both probe runs exited 0 at the product runner and turned the augmented oracle red. That violates the approved rule that a sign-time finding is conclusive only for the exact expected failure count/targets with a diagnostic rooted in the staged bundle and an inspected kind.

The claimed survivor rulings are:

- **R8, control ignores kind: not equivalent.** The contract explicitly makes a wrong kind inconclusive. A field-isolated reachable row can have fake codesign select the pinned stray from its bytes, then make the leg's later `file` inspection report `file-macho`; path, mode 0644, and the pinned SHA-256 remain unchanged. The current exact comparison rejects it; an ignore-kind classifier accepts it. Add this row and make R8 red.
- **R9, control ignores order: equivalent under this leg.** Signing is synchronous, every target is signed once, and the app call is unconditionally last; with exactly the executable and app failing, their evidence order cannot differ. R19 independently changes that producer invariant and is killed by eight rows.
- **M18, unsorted recursive walk: equivalent.** Every scan collection is sorted before collision checking, plan publication, mutation order, and `LINK` output; boolean ancestry tests are order-independent. I independently removed the inner `sorted(os.listdir(d))` at the exact green and the isolated 26-test relocation suite still passed 26/26.

The production facts support that boundary. `configureAgentNet`, `configureAgentHelper`, and `configureAgentLogin` still compile-time return outside Linux, so no Darwin browser path calls `agent_helper.launch` or `agent_login.launch`. The only Darwin production spawn is the separate, synchronous setup command: `runAgentSetupMacos` uses `zb_agent_spawn_group`, passes the browser pid to setup, and the setup process itself arms the kqueue parent watch and kills its own group. It also does not call `zb_spawn_policy_linux(0)` today. Threading a new value through setup would therefore prove the wrong consumer and would not satisfy “the production Darwin launch may not retain `zb_spawn_policy_linux(0)`.” That sentence is a constraint on the future helper/login caller, not a defect in the present setup path.

Slice 2a's interface direction is acceptable with these exact conditions:

1. Replace the globally required socket directory with one explicit tagged realization owned by the launch builder: Linux carries its required `net_dir` and optional e2e bwrap override; Darwin carries its validated nonzero `u16` egress port. The same rule applies to the e2e whole-host-command override: accepting `ZIGBROWSER_AGENT_HOST_CMD` on Darwin would bypass the reviewed `zb_agent_host launch`/Seatbelt consumer, so it must be refused before spawn. The existing non-e2e “ignored” warning behavior may remain. Darwin test-CA and login CLI inputs, if supported, must reach the real Python parser rather than disappear.

An unused compatibility shim that merely rejects the now-invalid composition adds no proof. The one source of Darwin argv must be `agent_helper.launch`/`agent_login.launch` themselves.
4. The tiny Zig executable is acceptable only as a bridge over the real production builders. It must emit the complete argv, assert the exact prefix `[python, "-m", "zb_agent_host", "launch"]`, and pass the remaining argument vector to the real Python `launch.main`; a bare unexplained `argv[4:]` is not a stable contract. Drive chat and login separately at both 51234 and 40001, require no Darwin `--net-dir`/`--bwrap`, and prove the real Python consumer carries the same port through its lease to the inner helper. Patching `LAUNCH_SPEC_CONSUMABLE()` true is permitted only in this hostless test seam and must not create a product bypass. Drive the Linux argv through the real Linux `launch.main`/exec seam and compare the full result with the frozen references.
5. The green must register the cross-language row in aggregate `test` with no skip mapping. A prefix/suffix mistake, a chat-only implementation, or a login-only implementation must also turn the registered step red.

This proof may be described as “the Zig builder composes with the real Python consumer.” It may not be described as a built production Darwin connector, close A-W1/MS6.8, or upgrade the plan status beyond a driven seam with no production caller. Under R30.0/R1, 4pa.21 stays open until 4pa.27/MS8 makes the browser's Darwin helper and login path call these builders and the real spawn/stop lifecycle.

Readiness remains exactly two derived facts, not a new flag: Zig's eventual production boundary uses `agent_setup.seatbelt_ready` through the existing platform gate before netd/helper/profile/spawn work, and Python uses `sandbox_macos.LAUNCH_SPEC_CONSUMABLE()` before its lease. ## 2026-10-23 — 4pa.80 slice-2 audit-classifier pre-red plan

**APPROVED TO WRITE THE HOSTLESS UNREGISTERED RED, WITH THE AMENDMENTS BELOW.** The corrected evidence vector is authoritative: Intel has **11 deltas** (TS 6, AR 5) from 22 before/after lines; arm64 has **18 deltas** (TS 11, AR 1, one PU-parent change, five PU additions) from 31 lines. The red must pin those per-family and total counts and reproduce the full retained Intel and arm64 hashes after reversing only the declared root substitutions. This ruling approves a red design, not a green or a Mac run. The 3ui.13 hold remains absolute.

The exact TS pathname list is the correct closure. Implement it as literal reviewed entries, not a prefix, basename, `com.apple.*`, daemon-name, `TemporaryItems`, or regex family. The `+32 × additions` Usage-parent rule is acceptable only as a coupled, bounded shape: each child is a before-absent/after-present regular file with uppercase ASCII UUID grammar, mode `100600`, size 102 or 115; the same Usage directory remains a directory with the same mode and inode and grows by exactly `32 * N`; there are no other changes below Usage; and **`1 <= N <= 5`**, the observed maximum. Zero additions, more than five, a parent-only change, a child-only change, or any extra Usage delta is `AUDIT-UNKNOWN`. A future legitimate larger cohort may be reviewed from new evidence; it may not silently widen this classifier.

The root contract needs two corrections before the red is satisfactory:

1. `given` may differ from `physical` because macOS intentionally resolves `/var` through `/private/var`. Require `physical == realpath/pwd -P(given)` and snapshot only separator-bounded physical paths. `link=yes` may mean only that the **terminal root entry itself** is a symlink and must then be rejected; it must not mean that a trusted ancestor such as `/var` resolved differently. Add a fixture and mutant for this exact `/var` alias so the real Mac roots are not rejected or accepted without verification.
2. State the complete canonical containment matrix. The only allowed nesting is `CACHES == HOME/Library/Caches` and `RUN` as a direct physical child of HOME named `zb-gate-<exact 40-hex commit>-<exact 16-hex nonce>`. RUN must be disjoint from TEMP, CACHE and CACHES; TEMP and CACHE must be disjoint from HOME/CACHES and from each other; no other equality or ancestor relation is allowed. The plan's phrase “RUN nested with any surface” cannot include HOME because RUN is intentionally its child. Assign snapshot paths by the most-specific physical root (CACHES before HOME), using path-component boundaries, and reject ambiguity.

Root declarations alone are not traversal proof. Each before and after snapshot must independently establish non-vacuous coverage of TEMP, CACHE, CACHES and shallow HOME; preserve and update the existing K4 both-snapshot/four-surface row. Add a mutant that omits an entire surface from both snapshots—identical omission must not become `AUDIT-CLEAN`. Missing/duplicate roots, malformed/unsorted/duplicate snapshot rows, an unowned path, a nonphysical path, a RUN path, or any structural classifier error must make the classifier nonzero and the runner `INCONCLUSIVE`, never CLEAN or AMBIENT.

Keep the change rules closed and named. TS requires the exact path and unchanged type/mode/inode/size with only mtime changing. AR requires an exact reviewed file path, regular-file shape, unchanged mode and size, and a changed inode. PU is the bounded coupled rule above. Deletion, an addition outside PU, a broader prefix, type/mode/size mismatch, unreviewed inode change, unexpected timestamp change, or run-root alias remains UNKNOWN. Use named immutable rule tables/IDs and separate parsing, validation, delta derivation, classification, and output; do not bury I/O or mutation in classifier conditions. The first-unknown detail must come from the deterministic normalized order.

At the runner boundary, AMBIENT is nonblocking only if the driver contains exactly one `PHASE<TAB>canaries<TAB>ok<TAB>3 refused`, `denied=no`, every required raw evidence file exists, the classifier exits zero, and the printed SHA-256 matches the complete `audit.classified`. Missing/wrong canary proof, any denial, classifier error, missing/changed classified evidence, or UNKNOWN is INCONCLUSIVE. Preserve the ambient-plus-denial and K10 UNKNOWN rows, and add a missing-classifier/changed-classification integration row.

Define the evidence manifest without self-reference: generate it only after classification, over one sorted relative-path inventory of every required regular evidence file (including driver output, `audit.roots`, both raw snapshots and `audit.classified`), exactly once; exclude the manifest itself and reject nonregular/duplicate evidence. The hostless oracle must prove that omitting or changing `audit.classified` breaks the manifest check. The Mac request, if later approved, must return the literal classified deltas, `audit.classified` hash and this manifest.

Preserve every accepted slice-1 helper/layout row and all containment K1-K14, ff, prep, transport, evidence-before-remove and fail-closed cleanup proofs. The green must register both new red oracles and all fixture/provenance/classifier inputs under the existing aggregate step with no skip mapping, then run the submitted blanket mutants through that registered step. No browser/WebKit/XPC exception is created: AMBIENT means only “matches a reviewed pathname/metadata shape,” not process attribution. Any denial or unreviewed activity remains inconclusive.

The commit adds exactly the disclosed executable Python file, changes no existing file, is absent from `build.zig`, passes `py_compile` and `git diff --check`, and deletes no test. From a clean exact archive under `env -i` and private HOME/TMP, `python3 -X dev tests/e2e/mac_native_close_binding_test.py` exited 1 with exactly the seven disclosed N1–N4 problems. Its lexer is the already reviewed 4pa.55 comment/literal-stripping lexer. In a disposable exact-tree probe, the proposed NSWindowDelegate conformance, post-allocation delegate assignment, exact `windowWillClose:`/`webViewDidClose:` forwarding, shared deferred registry-checked destroy path, and pre-`orderOut:` detach made this oracle pass; the existing 4pa.55 timer-binding oracle also remained green.

The N1 proof is not causal enough. I then moved only `_window.delegate = self;` above `_window = [[NSWindow alloc] ...]`. Objective-C sends the first assignment to nil, so the newly allocated window has no delegate and its native close still cannot reach `windowWillClose:`. The complete proposed oracle nevertheless printed PASS. N1 checks only that assignment and allocation occur in the same method, not their execution order or a later override. That is a proof which can pass the original product defect under R3.

The primary-window question is answered by the session's standing R30.3 owner default: **native close matches Linux**. Linux's driven lifecycle row sends `WM_DELETE_WINDOW` to the primary; destruction removes it from the registry, and removing the last window exits the browser. If another popup remains, Linux removes the primary and keeps the remaining window/application alive. This is distinct from automation `page.close` and script `window.close()`, which continue to refuse/ignore the primary. Therefore the eventual native-close production path must not silently return for `self == app.mainWindow`. The current red may keep its popup rows, but it cannot register a green that permanently encodes the opposite primary behavior.

Exact fix-forward minimum:

1. Strengthen N1 so the exact `_window.delegate = self;` is after the allocating `_window = [[NSWindow alloc] ...]` expression and before the initializer's final `return self;`. Census the host-window delegate assignments so the only permitted assignments are that binding and the N4 `self.window.delegate = nil;` in `-close`; a pre-allocation binding or an intervening override must turn the oracle red.
2. Apply the binding default for native primary close. The shared registered-window destroy path must remove a primary too; `removeWindow:` already requests exit when it removes the last window. Preserve the existing automation-primary refusal and script-primary-ignore contracts.
3. Add failing-first Mac runtime coverage, not a lexical claim, for both distinguishing primary outcomes: native-close the primary while a popup remains (primary leaves `page.list`, popup stays routable, process lives), and native-close the last primary (WINCOUNT reaches zero and the process exits cleanly). Keep the existing popup native-close red and its accepted vector. These Mac rows remain held until 3ui.13 is approved and a run is separately approved/released.
4. Preserve N2–N4, the seven-problem red control, the eight submitted popup-path mutants, and the 4pa.55 binding oracle. At green, register the hostless oracle beside `test-load-failure-policy` and satisfy the existing `7f8b217` safety condition: extend `mac_ms2a_hook_inert_test.py` for hook 14, with the guard-drop mutant red.

The hostless oracle remains an R1 tripwire. Even after this red fix-forward, AppKit compilation and native-close behavior require the released both-Mac proof; neither a lexical green nor a cross-compile closes 4pa.58.

From a clean exact archive initialized as a scratch repository, under `env -i`, private HOME/TMP/XDG paths and a private user/network namespace, `zig build -j4 test-mac-bundle-layout-spike --summary all` exited 0 with **10/10 steps**; the audit unit suite passed **27/27**, and every registered layout, containment, audit, relocation, prep, and fetch/venv row remained green. In a separate fresh-cache copy, deleting only `== "Directory"` from `bundle_layout_spike_audit.py`'s PU-parent predicate made that same registered command exit 1 with **8/10 steps**. The sole unit failure was the new same-non-Directory subtest: the mutant returned `AUDIT-AMBIENT` instead of the required `AUDIT-UNKNOWN`. That is the exact causal mutant required by `ceaf2f23`; the prior ruling explicitly did not require a full 55-mutant rerun for this test-only repair.

This approval clears the slice-specific `fec3ff83` oracle block only. The exact ancestry tip is **not push-safe** because its immediate parent `50f215e5` remains blocked by `c6125315`, and it also descends from the separately blocked 3ui.13 green `e4b524e6`. The 3ui.13 all-Mac-run hold remains in force. No Mac contact or run release is authorized by this ruling.

## 2026-10-23 — 4pa.73 / MS1c two-Mac `.app` harness plan

**NEEDS REVISION; DO NOT IMPLEMENT THE PLAN AS WRITTEN.** A hostless unregistered red may proceed after the contract below is incorporated, but no green, registration, or Mac request is approved until the owner's pending crash-report decision is incorporated. The 3ui.13 hold independently forbids all Mac contact.

The central artifact contract is currently wrong. R30-MS1 Red 2 and the already approved unregistered universal red `89fa9c2` require one `.dmg` whose bundled executable, shim, and every other Mach-O are fat with both x86_64 and arm64 slices. The proposal instead invokes today's single-host `build-dmg.sh` once per Mac, creates two unrelated thin images, and checks only each host's native slice. That cannot prove the shipping artifact is universal and permits different bytes on the two Macs. The corrected harness must invoke the final universal `build-dmg.sh` once at the exact commit, record its SHA-256, copy those exact bytes to both run roots, and require the same digest on both hosts. Each leg must census every Mach-O in the unpacked app and require both architectures, run `codesign --verify --strict --deep`, and prove the executable's `otool -L` shim reference resolves to the bundled regular file. The separate `mac_build` roots may supply the exact-commit tests and fixtures, but all runnable `zig-out` product outputs must be removed before the workload and no test may fall back to them. Invoke `build-dmg.sh` as a subprocess from an isolated clean checkout so its EXIT trap and fixed local DMG name cannot overwrite the outer harness's cleanup state.

The mount/resource contract also needs to be explicit. Capture the attached device and exact mountpoint from `hdiutil attach -readonly -nobrowse -noautoopen`; install a trap that detaches it on every later path; copy the app only inside the run root; and never call `find -delete` while the image remains mounted. A detach failure is INCONCLUSIVE, retains the run root and evidence, and holds that host. Evidence must be copied before removal. Product failures (bad signature, inventory/arch/reference mismatch, workload failure, or an accepted missing-dylib control) are FAIL; safety/infrastructure failures (guard not active, leaked process, audit missing, detach/remove failure, or outside-root write) are INCONCLUSIVE. PASS requires both legs, the same universal DMG digest, complete evidence, no leak, and successful cleanup.

**Question 1 — reject `sitecustomize.py` as the sole SIGKILL boundary.** It is an implicit startup hook, does not by itself prove that every one of the four Python commands loaded the guard, and supplies no process-group identity or durable leak/host-hold behavior. Reuse the explicit `tests/e2e/mac_term_teardown.py` contract by adapting the M9 runner and `exec_arch_probe.py`, or use an equally explicit committed wrapper that each command invokes directly. Every browser must start in its own process group; teardown sends SIGTERM only; a survivor emits `LEAK<TAB>pid<TAB>pgid<TAB>start<TAB>command`, writes a durable LEAKED marker before cleanup, retains the run root, stops further rows on that host, and makes the leg INCONCLUSIVE. Cover direct `os.kill`/`os.killpg` SIGKILL, `Popen.kill`, `Popen.send_signal(SIGKILL)`, and `subprocess.run` timeout behavior, including the screenshot path. If an interpreter guard remains as defense in depth, require a per-command activation record and mutants for a missing `PYTHONPATH`/wrapper, a restored `.kill()`, cleanup after a leak, and continuing to the next row after a leak. No refused SIGKILL may be followed by run-root deletion.

**Question 2 — set the private environment, and every real-HOME delta is blocking.** Run each workload command with `HOME`, `CFFIXED_USER_HOME`, `TMPDIR`, relevant XDG/cache variables, profile/socket/capture paths, and Python temporary paths under that leg's run root. Because these are `--e2e-protocol` runs, also set the reviewed `ZB_DEBUG_EPHEMERAL_DATA_STORE=1` hook to reduce persistent WebKit data. Preserve the physical account home separately for auditing. Snapshot it recursively before and after while excluding only the exact run-root subtree; report every added, changed, or deleted path. Any delta outside the run root is INCONCLUSIVE regardless of whether its name mentions zigBrowser, `com.cosmichq`, or an unrelated system daemon. The owner exception covers only that user's `/var/folders`; it does not create an ambient-write allowlist for `~/Library`. No cleanup may touch the real home outside the run root.

For `/var/folders`, resolve and record the physical per-user roots from both `DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR` and `DARWIN_USER_CACHE_DIR`, take deterministic recursive before/after snapshots, and include every added or changed path in the evidence without filtering or deletion. A `/var/folders` delta alone is reportable and may coexist with PASS under the owner decision; a missing/incomplete snapshot cannot. Add hostless rows and causal mutants for a filtered delta, a dropped snapshot, and any delete target under those roots.

The negative missing-dylib control remains pending **Question 3's owner decision**. Do not freeze its accepted outcome or launch it until that decision arrives. If approved, bind the control to a private copy inside the run root, require nonzero exit plus the exact bundled shim load failure, retain stderr/exit status, and apply the same SIGTERM/leak and audit boundaries. If the owner denies DiagnosticReports writes, an unsandboxed executed control is not permissible; replace it only through a separately reviewed containment method or revise the plan explicitly.

The hostless oracle must drive the real corrected harness with executing transport/tool stubs inside a private network namespace. In addition to the proposed rows, require mutants for two different/thin DMGs, a non-fat nested Mach-O, use of `zig-out` after deletion, missing `--strict --deep`, an un-detached mount, cleanup after leak/detach failure, an ignored external-HOME delta, missing `/var/folders` evidence, one-leg PASS, and a control with the wrong diagnostic. Keep the failure path flat: collect each command's status on its own line, classify it with a bounded named outcome, then decide cleanup; do not hide effecting commands or assignments in conditionals.

## 2026-10-23 — 4pa.59 all-gate provenance RED `189cb320`

**BLOCK.** Exact `189cb3203ca312856b86aefd7602e1f067ff03ec` is a useful unregistered red and its reported baseline is honest, but its oracle does not prove the stated property that the bytes shipped by each gate equal the exact commit it names, nor does it behaviorally prove fresh-root refusal. This SHA is not approved as the 4pa.59 red.

The commit adds only `tests/mac/mac_gate_provenance_selftest.sh`, is absent from `build.zig`, and is clean under `git diff --check`. Its 30-row census exactly matches the runnable caller list in the registered Mac-configuration oracle; the destructive reaper is not run. The outer test and every gate invocation execute behind private user/network namespaces with `ssh` and `scp` stubs first on PATH, explicit TEST-NET hosts and a private fake key, so no Mac or external network is reachable. From a clean detached clone of the exact commit under `env -i` and private HOME/TMP, the test exited 1 in about 20 seconds with exactly **26 `FAIL` rows**, matching the disclosure. There are **18 `ok` lines** because the 17 passing gates are preceded by the dirty-tree setup control.

The blocking issue is that P1 searches only for one appended marker. It never compares a shipped tracked file or archive member with `git show "$SHA:<path>"`, so marker-free wrong bytes are accepted as exact-commit bytes. In an exact-tree probe, `build-on-mac.sh` still created the named commit archive but appended a second `build.zig` tar member containing deliberately different, marker-free bytes. The red continued to print `ok   build-on-mac: ships only commit ...`; all eight existing build-on-mac callers also remained green and the total stayed at the baseline 26 failures. P4 cannot repair this: it merely greps the first run's combined output for the requested SHA and has no binding to the captured payload.

P2 is likewise a narrow textual pattern, not a freshness proof. Replacing the reviewed plain create with `d="$HOME/$RDIR"; mkdir -p "$d"` evades its regex while retaining the unsafe reuse semantics. The exact red again printed the build-on-mac provenance row green with the same 26-failure total. P3 only observes two different random names, so it does not demonstrate that a pre-existing selected root is refused. Separately, P0/P1/P2/P4 are evaluated only for the first invocation; the second invocation contributes only its directory names to P3. A gate that ships dirty bytes only on its second invocation stays green. The payload scanner also returns after the first valid tar embedded in a stream despite claiming to inspect every tar, and P0 does not bind a payload to each configured host.

Exact fix-forward minimum:

1. Preserve the exact 30-gate census, unregistered status, private network namespaces, executing transport stubs, current 26-failure control and the working-tree-marker rows, but add an **exact-byte binding**. Every captured tracked payload and every tracked member of every shipped/embedded archive must be matched by path and bytes to the named commit. Explicitly classify generated/pinned artifacts; an unknown or unmapped payload fails closed. A mutant that replaces or appends one tracked archive member with different marker-free bytes while still printing the requested SHA must turn the red oracle red.
2. Inspect all payload containers, not just the first tar found in a stream. Add a control with a clean first archive and a dirty or wrong-byte second archive, and require it to fail. Preserve recursive directory/scp coverage.
3. Make fresh-root proof behavioral. Drive a selected run-root name that already exists in the fake remote state and require the gate to refuse before extraction, build or other product work. Keep the two-run nonce-disjointness row, and add an indirect-variable `d=...; mkdir -p "$d"` mutant; lexical matching of one `$HOME` spelling is insufficient.
4. Apply the non-vacuity, exact-payload, fresh-root and printed-SHA assertions to **both** invocations, and bind at least one exact payload to each configured host. Add a second-invocation-only dirty-payload mutant. P4 must use a bounded, documented commit/result line and be checked per invocation; a free SHA appearing anywhere in combined output is not evidence of what was shipped.

At green, register the strengthened oracle with `has_side_effects` in `test-mac-build-script` and aggregate `test`, preserve the destructive-reaper exclusion, and run the marker, marker-free wrong-member, indirect-`mkdir -p`, second-invocation and second-archive mutants through that registered step. No Mac run is needed to review this hostless red/green boundary, and this ruling authorizes no Mac contact.
