This ruling reviews only the two files added by this commit; it does not adjudicate parent red `143a12f6` and authorizes no Mac contact.

The disclosed baselines reproduce. From a clean exact archive under `env -i`, private HOME/TMP and the tests' own private namespaces, `mac_m9_term_only_test.py` exited 1 with all **6/6 cases** red for the stated current behavior: shared process groups, SIGKILL in the GUI and screenshot paths, no group-directed SIGTERM, and no fail-closed leak result. `ms1c_app_gate_selftest.sh` exited 1 with exactly **29/29 `FAIL` rows**, correcting the commit message's stale 28-row count. Its transport stubs reject unknown hosts and run behind a private network namespace, so neither execution could reach a real Mac or external network. The commit adds no build step, changes no production file and is clean under `git diff --check`.

The blocker is independently reproducible in `judge()`. A synthetic ignore-case transcript containing the required `killpg(browser_pgid, SIGTERM)` plus an additional direct `kill(unrelated_pid, SIGTERM)`, and **two** `LEAK` records whose pgids and identity fields are wrong, returns an empty problem list. The current code rejects only non-TERM signal numbers, not TERM delivered through the wrong operation or to the wrong target; then it accepts any leak count and checks only that at least one record has the browser pid and five or more fields. Thus a green can retain unsafe direct signaling and fabricate or duplicate the durable identity evidence while the registered proof remains green.

The gate oracle has the corresponding identity gap. F8 checks a hard-coded pid/pgid prefix followed by `.*`; it does not require exactly one workload `LEAK` record, validate its pgid/start/command fields, or prove that the retained `LEAKED` marker is the byte-for-byte durable form of that observed identity. The base workload row also counts four invocations without requiring the exact four distinct scripts, and P2 binds only a reported tree id rather than the full named commit. Those are material parts of the approved MS1c contract, not optional formatting.

Exact fix-forward minimum:

1. In the real-path term-only oracle, require every nonzero recorded signal to be exactly `killpg(<browser pgid>, SIGTERM)`; reject direct `kill`, another process/group, and duplicate or additional TERM sends unless a specifically reviewed protocol requires them. Permit signal 0 only as a liveness probe and bind it to the browser identity. Add field-isolated transcripts/mutants for an extra direct TERM and a TERM to the wrong group.
2. For an ignoring browser, require exactly one `LEAK<TAB>pid<TAB>pgid<TAB>start<TAB>command` record, with pid and pgid equal to the observed browser, nonempty validated start/command identity, exit 1, and exactly one `FAIL<TAB>mac-teardown-leak` result. Add duplicate-LEAK, wrong-pgid, wrong-start and wrong-command controls. Cooperative cases must continue to require zero leak records and a reaped browser.
3. In F8, capture the actual workload `LEAK` identity and require exactly one retained `LEAKED` marker with the same four identity fields; keep HOST_STOPPED, no later row/control, no cleanup, retained root and the next-run HOST_HELD proof. A hard-coded or mismatched marker must turn the oracle red.
4. Strengthen P5 to require exactly one invocation of each of `m9_render_test.py`, `m9_reply_channel_test.py`, `m9_load_fail_test.py` and `exec_arch_probe.py` per leg, while retaining the browser/path/environment checks. Strengthen P2/evidence to name and compare the full requested commit SHA as well as its tree and universal-DMG digest.

Keep the present 29 gate rows, universal one-DMG/same-bytes requirement, every-Mach-O fat census, strict/deep verification, loader-reference check, mount/detach safety, private environment, complete home and `/var/folders` audits, narrow crash-report exception, skip-as-fail controls, manifest and fail-closed cleanup. The eventual green also depends on the reviewed universal `build-dmg.sh` and real shared `mac_varfolders_audit.sh`, must register both oracles without a skip mapping, and must pass the registered Mac configuration and direct-transport censuses so the new gate cannot bypass the one config helper or pinned transport. No Mac runtime request follows from an unregistered red.

## 2026-10-23 — shared `/var/folders` audit RED `143a12f6`

**BLOCK.** Exact `143a12f69af9dac126581026cf4ca41fc7b0b52f` is correctly unregistered, hostless and failing first, but it does not yet prove two central claims in its own contract: that the helper performs no transient write/delete under the audited roots, and that every content change is observable. This SHA is not approved as the shared audit red and is not push-safe. No Mac contact is authorized.

The baseline is honest. The commit adds only `tests/e2e/mac_varfolders_audit_test.py`, changes no build step or production file and is clean under `git diff --check`. From a clean exact archive under `env -i` and private HOME/TMP, it exits 1 with the one disclosed problem: `tests/mac/mac_varfolders_audit.sh` does not exist. Running the same exact oracle against the submitter's extant scratch helper passes V1–V8, confirming that the test is satisfiable and that the stale base SHA in the commit message is only documentary.

The first causal survivor is a helper that creates and deletes a file below the physical temp root during root discovery, before taking its snapshot. I inserted exactly `: > "$pt/.zb-vf-transient"; rm -f -- "$pt/.zb-vf-transient"` into the otherwise passing draft helper. The oracle still printed `PASS mac-varfolders-audit V1-V8`. V8 compares only the final regular-file map; it cannot observe a transient create/delete, and it ignores directories and other filesystem entry types. That contradicts the owner rule and the test's explicit statement that the helper writes and deletes nothing there.

The second survivor is a real content change with unchanged fields. The draft snapshot records kind, mode, size, mtime, inode and link target, but no regular-file content digest or ctime. After `varfolders_begin`, rewriting `cache.db` from `v1\n` to the same-length `v2\n` and restoring its original nanosecond mtime made `varfolders_end` return exit 0 with `VARFOLDERS COMPLETE 0` and no delta. The committed rows only use a different-length rewrite, so a helper with this incomplete snapshot remains green despite missing a changed path.

There are two related binding gaps. V3/V4 compare only each delta's class, token and relative path; they do not require the before/after fields to be present or equal the observed snapshot metadata. V1 proves one static symlink resolution but no row changes the getconf answers or replaces a physical root between begin and end, so dropping root continuity/device-inode binding is not driven by the red.

Exact fix-forward minimum:

1. Add a mutation-observation boundary for both physical roots and all descendants that makes a transient create/delete or write/restore by the helper itself red. Linux inotify/syscall observation, or an equivalently causal mechanism, is acceptable. Cover regular files, directories, symlinks and other entry types; a final-state regular-file dictionary is not sufficient. Retain the no-delete mutants for both begin and end.
2. Make the snapshot detect a same-size regular-file content rewrite even when mtime and inode are preserved. A content digest is the direct proof; a different scheme is acceptable only with a driven equivalent row. Add corresponding symlink-target and entry-type/metadata changes so “every changed path” is concrete rather than size/mtime-only.
3. Parse the full DELTA record in the oracle. Require exactly six tab-separated fields, exact before/after values derived from the snapshots (`-` only on the absent side), deterministic ordering, one roots line, one final COMPLETE/INCONCLUSIVE line and a count equal to the exact delta set. Structural or snapshot errors must remain exit 2 with no partial deltas presented as complete.
4. Bind the root identity across the run. Record each resolved root's device/inode at begin, re-resolve at end, and make changed getconf output, a replaced root or a symlink retarget INCONCLUSIVE. Add rows/mutants for each. Preserve the current failing/empty/missing/same-root and unreadable-directory controls.

The green must then add the one shared sourced helper, register this strengthened oracle and mutant pass without a skip mapping, and drive both consumers: the runtime-rows Mac leg and the MS1c gate. Each consumer must treat INCONCLUSIVE as non-pass and include the complete unfiltered output in copied evidence. This review does not approve either consumer or any Mac run.

## 2026-10-23 — 4pa.56 cookie-INFLIGHT hostless GREEN `46950f35`

**APPROVED ON ITS OWN CONTENT AS THE HOSTLESS GREEN; MAC CLOSURE REMAINS PENDING.** Exact `46950f35b7c80bdce710c8172d0db267fcbf5167` satisfies the hostless production and registration conditions recorded for red fix-forward `0907b353` in `4dbc8378`. This ruling does not approve a Mac run or cure the blocked intervening ancestry.

The production placement is correct in all three functions. `zb_cookies_get_all`, `zb_cookies_add` and `zb_cookies_delete` each call `zb_job_inflight(job)` exactly once, immediately after the complete `WKHTTPCookieStore` submit, inside the same unique main-queue block and after its negated `zb_job_claim_dispatch(job)` guard. The transition closes the actual wait hole: the waiter can remain unbounded only while the job is DISPATCHING, whereas the post-submit INFLIGHT state is deadline-bound. It is also safe if a completion happens before the new line: `zb_job_complete` may move DISPATCHING directly to DONE, and the later `zb_job_inflight` changes only DISPATCHING to INFLIGHT, so it cannot overwrite DONE or resurrect an abandoned job.

Registration is additive and fail-closed. `test-cookie-inflight-binding` runs the approved real-shim oracle, declares the oracle, imported lexer and shim as file inputs, and is a direct dependency of aggregate `test`; the red oracle's executable assertions are unchanged, with only its registration documentation updated. No existing step input was removed.

From a clean exact archive under `env -i`, private HOME/TMP/XDG paths, Zig's default caches and a private current-user/network/PID/mount namespace, `zig build -j4 test-cookie-inflight-binding test-native-close-binding test-load-failure-policy cross-check --summary all` exited 0 with **32/32 steps and 7/7 tests**. The named cookie oracle passed K1–K3 for all three operations; the adjacent native-close and load-failure checks remained green; cross-check compiled both Mac architectures and Windows at its existing contract boundary. No Mac or external network was contacted.

Two independent production probes through the registered step confirm causality. Removing only the get-all release made K1 and K2 fail. Inverting only the add operation's claim guard made K3 fail. Both commands exited 1 at the named rows, demonstrating that the registered proof binds both the transition and its guarded main-queue context rather than accepting the intended text elsewhere.

4pa.56 remains open for the mandatory Mac proof: compile the real Objective-C shim on both Mac architectures and run `mac_cookie_timeout_test.py` on both hosts from a separately reviewed exact ancestry. Any browser-launching request must use the accepted shared physical `/var/folders` audit boundary, report every added or changed path, delete nothing there, and retain the standing no-keychain, no-SIGKILL, no-install and per-run release requirements.

This commit descends through blocked reds `143a12f6` and `d22a985c`; approving its own content does not make that ancestry push-safe. No new global tip is named. The last named push-safe Mac tip remains `1a52e4cc`.

## 2026-10-23 — 4pa.59 all-gate provenance RED fix-forward `e92e0e4b`

**BLOCK.** Exact `e92e0e4bfc1dd1758b056ce46a6c5dc279d58a5a` substantially strengthens the useful red from `189cb320`, and its reported baseline is honest, but two causal holes remain in the exact-byte and fresh-root boundaries required by `a38b0667`. This SHA is not approved as the 4pa.59 red and is not a new push-safe tip. It remains unregistered and this ruling authorizes no Mac contact.

The exact clean-archive run under `env -i`, private HOME/TMP and the test's private user/network namespace exited 1 with exactly **27 failures**. It preserved the separately computed legacy count at 26, rejected the marker-free duplicate member in one archive, rejected a clean first archive followed by a wrong second archive only on invocation two, and classified the submitted single indirect `d=...; mkdir -p "$d"` control as unsafe. The 30-gate census remains complete, the destructive reaper is not run, both invocations and configured fake hosts are driven, and no real Mac or external network is reachable.

The first blocker is an unclassified-byte survivor in `exact_payloads`. Once `inspect_bytes` finds any tar set, it binds the tar members and returns without accounting for bytes before, between or after those archives. I copied the captured otherwise-exact `build-on-mac` invocation and prepended the marker-free command `echo marker-free-unclassified-prefix` to its valid tar stream. The exact-byte audit still returned success. This contradicts both the stated closed classification and the `a38b0667` requirement to inspect every shipped or embedded payload: arbitrary remote-program bytes can accompany an exact archive without being matched to the commit or an explicit generated/pinned class.

The second blocker is that P2 is still lexical rather than behavioral. `remote_model.py` extracts all root-looking names but then examines only `mkdir_args[0]` and decides refusal solely from that first command's `-p` flag; it never binds the mkdir operand to the selected pre-existing root or interprets the whole sequence. A direct control containing `mkdir "$HOME/unrelated-abcdef"; d="$HOME/zb-control-123abc"; mkdir -p "$d"; echo product` was reported as refused because the unrelated first mkdir was exclusive. The unsafe selected-root reuse and subsequent product command were therefore masked. A gate with this shape can satisfy P2 even though the pre-existing run root is reused.

Exact fix-forward minimum:

1. Make the payload audit consume the entire captured byte stream. Every byte range must be attributed to an exact committed payload/archive member or to a bounded, explicitly validated generated/pinned segment; tar framing/padding may be recognized structurally, but arbitrary prefix, suffix or bytes between concatenated archives must fail closed. Add marker-free unknown prefix, suffix and between-archives controls, while preserving the duplicate-member and second-invocation second-archive controls.
2. Bind fresh-root refusal to the selected run-root operand and the complete command sequence, not the first textual mkdir. An executing fake remote filesystem is preferred; an interpreter is acceptable only if it resolves assignments/quoting and every relevant existence/mkdir/product operation. Add the demonstrated earlier-unrelated-exclusive-mkdir plus later indirect `mkdir -p "$d"` mutant and require it to reach the unsafe/product record and turn the oracle red. Also retain positive controls for safe plain exclusive mkdir and an explicit pre-existence guard followed by `mkdir -p`, so the oracle proves refusal semantics rather than one mandated spelling.
3. Preserve the exact 27-failure production baseline, separate 26-failure legacy control, 30-gate census, destructive-reaper exclusion, both invocations, every configured host, per-host exact-payload non-vacuity, bounded result lines, nonce-disjoint roots and private network namespace. Keep the red unregistered; at green, register the strengthened oracle with `has_side_effects` in `test-mac-build-script` and aggregate `test`, with all causal controls run through that step.

The failing-first control is honest. From a clean exact archive under `env -i`, private HOME/TMP and a private current-user/network/PID/mount namespace, the oracle exited 1 with the one disclosed missing-helper problem. Running that exact oracle against the submitter's scratch draft helper passed V1–V11. The live create/delete probe makes this boundary non-vacuous.

The blocking survivor is the physical root itself. Both the test's `observe` and the draft helper's snapshot enumerate only descendants; each ROOT record contains device and inode but no ordinary metadata. I changed only the temp root's mode from 0755 to 0700 between begin and end. `varfolders_end` returned exit 0 with the correct roots line followed by `VARFOLDERS COMPLETE 0`. The physical root is a changed path under the reviewed snapshot surface, and the exact `30a7a1e8` minimum required type/mode and metadata changes to be concrete rather than silently excluded. The claimed strict output parser has two smaller but causal holes. `parse` accepts `VARFOLDERS<TAB>INCONCLUSIVE<TAB>` as a reason-bearing terminal record, and it blindly discards any first line beginning `VARFOLDERS_ROOTS<TAB>` when an inconclusive row does not pass an expected roots value. Direct probes therefore accepted both an empty reason and `VARFOLDERS_ROOTS<TAB>bogus`. Those are not the documented one roots record and nonempty reason, and they would make incomplete evidence look structurally valid.

Exact fix-forward minimum:

1. Include each physical T/C root's full auditable record in the before/after comparison while retaining the separate device/inode identity binding. A relative `.` representation or an explicitly typed root-delta form is acceptable if deterministic and unambiguous.
2. Make INCONCLUSIVE structure exact: exactly three tab-separated terminal fields with a nonempty reason. When a roots line is present, require exactly `VARFOLDERS_ROOTS<TAB>T<TAB><absolute physical T><TAB>C<TAB><absolute physical C>` and compare it with the known recorded roots for rows where they are available; malformed, duplicate or arbitrary roots lines must fail. Add empty-reason and malformed-roots mutants.
3. Preserve V1–V11, the live inotify proof, every-entry V8 comparison, same-size restored-mtime content row, link/type/mode/FIFO rows, exact six-field ordered delta comparison, root device/inode continuity, ambiguous-name refusal and all 23 submitted mutants. Keep the red unregistered. At green, register the strengthened oracle and mutant pass without a skip mapping and drive both runtime-rows and MS1c consumers, with INCONCLUSIVE remaining non-pass and complete unfiltered output copied into evidence.

## 2026-10-23 — shared `/var/folders` audit GREEN `1362d73a`

**BLOCK.** Exact `1362d73a2d176571a3da74325aa58f2782e7d560` correctly implements and registers the approved V1–V14 helper oracle and its 34-mutant pass, and its runtime-row integration treats an incomplete *final* audit as non-pass. It nevertheless starts browser work after a failed or incomplete **before** snapshot, and it does not yet drive the second required MS1c consumer. It therefore does not satisfy the complete green condition in `f0590a29`, is not sufficient to release 4pa.55 or another browser-launching Mac run, and is not a new push-safe tip. No Mac contact is authorized.

The submitted verification reproduces from a clean exact archive. Under `env -i`, private HOME/TMP/XDG, a private current-user/network/PID/mount namespace and Zig's default caches, `zig build -j4 test-mac-varfolders-audit test-mac-build-script --summary all` exited 0 with **16/16 steps**. V1–V14 passed; the unaltered helper and TMPDIR control were green; all 34 named helper mutants were red; the runtime-row caller rows VF1–VF3 and outer exit-5 mapping passed. The helper has no here-document, invokes `/usr/bin/python3 -I -B -X utf8` with TMPDIR in the evidence directory, emits the exact roots/delta/terminal grammar, and the build graph registers both oracles without a skip mapping.

The first blocker is a fail-open precondition, not a formatting issue. `varfolders_begin` deliberately converts both `__vf_roots` failure and a nonzero before-snapshot Python result into exit 0. A direct exact-commit probe with a failing `getconf` returned `BEGIN_RC=0` while leaving a zero-byte roots file and no before snapshot. The registered VF2 row confirms the production consequence: with root discovery forced to fail, the test row still runs and reaches `MATCH`; only the later `varfolders_end` changes the leg to exit 5. The owner exception requires a before and after snapshot for a browser-launching run. A later INCONCLUSIVE verdict cannot reconstruct paths written during a launch that began without the before image.

The second blocker is the incomplete shared-consumer binding. The runtime rows leg sources the exact-commit helper and is driven, but `tests/mac/ms1c_app_gate.sh` does not exist and no row drives it. The `f0590a29` green condition explicitly required both the runtime-row and MS1c consumers. This is allowed to land as the runtime-row portion after the first blocker is fixed, but it cannot be called the complete shared-helper green until the MS1c gate sources the same helper and its registered oracle drives success, begin refusal, end INCONCLUSIVE, leak/retention and complete unfiltered evidence.

Exact fix-forward minimum:

1. Make `varfolders_begin` fail closed when either physical-root discovery or the before snapshot fails or is incomplete. The runtime leg must convert that result to its bounded INCONCLUSIVE outcome and stop **before** writing RUNNING, starting any row, or launching a browser. Add rows for failed/empty getconf and an unreadable/incomplete initial tree that require zero row/browser invocations, plus a production mutant that restores the current ignore-and-run behavior.
2. Preserve V1–V14, exact output and identity binding, all 34 helper mutants, the TMPDIR control, VF1/VF3, final-audit exit 5, verbatim evidence and no-delete rule. A successfully completed before snapshot remains silent; a refused begin may print/store a bounded reason, but it must not be mistaken for a complete cycle.
3. Bind the same exact-commit helper into the MS1c consumer when that gate lands and drive it through the registered MS1c oracle. Until then, describe this as runtime-row integration only; it does not clear the MS1c dependency.

## 2026-10-23 — R3 push-safety audit from `1a52e4cc` through `9cf6e07e`

**BLOCK; THERE IS NO CURRENTLY VALID PUSH-SAFE MAC SHA.** The first prefix in the requested open range, `5628b7466fdfa861415b55d5e72842e5d818f673`, already inherits a newly demonstrated R3 product defect and red registered gate from before the baseline. Consequently the prior push-safe designation of `1a52e4ccee49f06d7e967a86942982164147e728` is withdrawn, and neither it nor any descendant through `9cf6e07e1cc28f20454c41f2fd3a7b5817f34a7e` is authorized for push under this audit. No Mac was contacted or authorized.

The failure is in the registered `test-mac-dmg-script` path and the production DMG builder itself. `packaging/macos/build-dmg.sh` runs under `set -euo pipefail` and checks the bundled dependency with `otool -L ... | grep -qF ...`. Once `grep -qF` sees the matching shim line, it may close its input before `otool` emits later dependency lines. A resulting producer SIGPIPE makes the pipeline nonzero even though the required reference was present, so the builder prints the missing-reference error and aborts a valid package. The registered fake `otool` has exactly this realistic shape: one write containing the matching shim reference, followed by a separate libSystem write.

The defect reproduced at exact content rather than by source inference:

- From a clean exact `9cf6e07e` archive under `env -i`, private HOME/TMP/XDG and a private current-user/network/PID/mount namespace, the combined registered command for native-close, hook 14, cookie-inflight, load-failure, varfolders, DMG, Mac-build and cross-check steps exited 1: **51/53 steps**, with only the legacy half of `test-mac-dmg-script` red. Its release run aborted at the `otool` check and produced five downstream D1-D4 failures. Every other named step passed, including cross-check, the 35-mutant varfolders gate and `test-mac-build-script`.
- The same exact DMG step passed alone at both `-j1` and `-j4`, establishing timing sensitivity rather than a permanently red fixture.
- Twenty concurrent exact-HEAD executions of `dmg_script_selftest.sh` produced **17 passes and 3 failures**; each failure aborted at the same matching-reference pipeline and then failed the same five assertions.
- This is not introduced by the universal-DMG green. One hundred concurrent executions from exact pre-universal prefix `5f1ce9a` produced **91 passes and 9 failures** with the same registered oracle. `git blame` places the production pipeline at `73b7f83d4`; therefore the defect also invalidates the inherited baseline rather than identifying `56f9f2cb` as its origin.

The exact fix-forward minimum is small:

1. Make production collect `otool -L` output while separately checking `otool`'s exit status, then search the completed output without an early-closing producer pipe. Keep the exact shim-reference and resolved-regular-file requirements.
2. Make a registered DMG row deterministic by having the fake `otool` emit the matching reference and then a delayed or sufficiently bounded later dependency write. The current product must fail that row; the fixed product must pass both ad-hoc and release runs.
3. Run a production mutant restoring the `otool | grep -qF` pipeline through `test-mac-dmg-script` and require it to fail. Re-run that step with ordinary default caches and parallelism, plus `test-mac-build-script`, before requesting a new aggregate push-safe ruling.

All other ancestry blocks named in this audit have reviewed fix-forwards: the temporary fail-open `1362d73a` interval is repaired by `9c33b3c3`; the 4pa.58 and 4pa.56 greens and their registered hostless gates are green; the MS1c, 4pa.59 and `/var/folders` reds remain unregistered with their approved corrections; and the universal/provenance content remains approved apart from this newly discovered inherited DMG pipeline defect. The standing per-run Mac release rules and owner exceptions are unchanged.

## 2026-10-23 — 4pa.55 two-Mac request at `65456067` (mail 8635)

**BLOCK; DO NOT CONTACT EITHER MAC.** The two selected green rows and their per-leg accepted vector are appropriate, both requested hosts are pinned, and the orchestrator confirms that `/home/maceo/vms/mac-m9/zb_mac_key` is intentionally the one explicit key for both accounts. The exact requested SHA is nevertheless not an acceptable release point, and the submitted command/evidence/cleanup procedure is not fail-closed.

The ancestry issue is direct. `6545606733aa5b7b08502eba69fda6472b0915f9` is an ancestor of, not a descendant of, the current reviewed push-safe tip `08ae295e3138cd9a1c8869f66a07dbc8861b0afa`; it therefore omits the six registered 3ui.16 producer/pipefail repairs. The request must instead name exact `08ae295e` or a separately reviewed push-safe descendant and recompute every exact-commit input value.

The release command is also unsafe as written. This workspace uses zsh, whose pipeline-status array is `$pipestatus`, not Bash's `${PIPESTATUS[@]}`; `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` expands empty in the submitted command. Even under Bash, the final `echo` makes the compound command return 0 after a failed gate. In addition, the command names private local `HOME` and `TMPDIR` paths that its setup never creates; `build-on-mac.sh` uses `mktemp` below that TMPDIR and would fail before contact. The revised command must create fresh mode-0700 local HOME/TMP paths first, refuse pre-existing run/log targets, capture the gate status without relying on the caller's shell or a status-losing `tee` pipeline, print it, and exit with it. Redirecting the gate to the retained log, capturing `$?`, then displaying the completed log is sufficient.

Two evidence/cleanup claims do not meet the standing run boundary. The fifteen-file list is a useful harness subset but is not the complete build input: `build-on-mac.sh` ships `git archive --format=tar "$COMMIT" -- src build.zig build.zig.zon zigbrowser.json tests packaging`. The revised preflight must record the exact commit/tree and SHA-256 plus byte size of that complete deterministic archive (or an equivalent complete member manifest), in addition to the focused harness hashes. More importantly, the current gate deletes `evidence/rows/*.out` and the helpers' before/after records with the remote root, so it does not copy complete evidence before cleanup. It must copy a deterministic evidence archive and its SHA-256 back to the Linux scratch area before removal; the console summary remains required but is not a substitute.

## 2026-10-23 — 3ui.14 silent-transport RED `2d8db303`

**BLOCK.** Exact `2d8db3036f30910178e6178dfd6d1b56b1118361` is an honest, contained failing-first observation, but its verdict is not causally bound to the missing remote success evidence. It accepts any nonzero exit after both fake hosts have appeared, including an unconditional failure, an unrelated failure or its outer timeout. This SHA is not approved as the 3ui.14 red and is not ready to become a registered green oracle. No Mac was contacted or authorized.

The scope and baseline otherwise check out. The commit adds exactly one executable shell file, changes no production file, is absent from `build.zig`, has SHA-256 `581649dee49fa45b65582732b406f842d342e94d72ee59ed029fda141605f9d2`, passes `bash -n` and `git diff --check`, and runs only executing ssh/scp stubs inside a private network namespace. The stubs consume their input, emit no bytes and return 0; the destructive reaper gate is never run. From a clean exact archive under `env -i` with private HOME/TMP, both transport controls passed and all **20/20** named gates reached both fake hosts, returned 0 and printed their top-level both-arches PASS, so the oracle exited 1 in the submitted red shape.

The demonstrated survivor changes no transport or evidence check: in each of the twenty scratch gate scripts I changed only its single `overall=0` initialization to `overall=1`. Both host paths and every empty successful transport still ran, but each gate then exited 1 without its top-level PASS. The unmodified oracle exited 0 with `PASS mac-gate-empty-output-selftest 20/20`. No gate checked a remote-owned success line; they merely failed unconditionally. The same predicate `RC != 0 && top_pass == 0` would accept exit 124, a signal, or a failure in unrelated cleanup after the transports. Thus the current red cannot distinguish the intended fix from making every gate unusable.

Exact fix-forward minimum:

1. For every one of the twenty gates, bind the empty-output case to its own exact missing-remote-success refusal after the intended leg call on **each** fake host. Require the bounded ordinary refusal status (not timeout/signal/preflight failure), the exact two host/architecture refusal records, and no leg or top-level PASS. Merely observing some ssh/scp call to each host is not enough.
2. Add a non-vacuous positive mode for every gate: the transport supplies that gate's exact remote-owned success record at the intended leg boundary, both legs succeed, and the gate exits 0 with exactly one expected top-level both-arches PASS. A success record emitted by a build/stage/cleanup transport call, a wrong record or a record for only one architecture must not satisfy the gate.
3. Preserve the zero-byte/status-0 ssh and scp controls, all twenty current rows, private HOME/TMP and network namespace, and the destructive-reaper exclusion. Drive at least the demonstrated unconditional-`overall=1` survivor, restored empty-output acceptance, wrong-call/wrong-marker success and timeout/nonzero-unrelated failure through the oracle; each must be red for its causal row.
4. Keep the red unregistered. At green, register it under the appropriate Mac build-script and aggregate steps without a skip mapping and run one production marker-check mutant per distinct gate shape. `ms_runtime_rows_gate.sh` remains expressly out of this twenty-gate ownership slice, but 3ui.14 cannot close or claim every known affected gate until its owner supplies equivalent empty-output refusal and positive-control proof.

**BLOCK.** Exact `a321ae5c540e4ec804804cb0ebdc7b87039430b7` correctly registers and satisfies the approved signal, universal-artifact, mount, workload, control, evidence and ordinary audit rows, but its real-account-HOME snapshot can miss a content change and still return the two-leg top-level PASS. R and J, all six real signal/teardown cases, P1–P9 and F1–F20 were green; the one-DMG digest, exact fat census, strict/deep verification, loader reference, four distinct workloads, crash-control binding, shared `/var/folders` output, evidence manifest, detach/leak retention and successful normal cleanup were all exercised.

The causal survivor is in `ms1c_app_gate.sh`'s independent `snap()` implementation. A regular-file record contains only path, type, mode, inode, size and second-resolution mtime; it contains neither a content digest nor ctime. I added one field-isolated hostless row to the unchanged oracle scaffold: before the leg, an existing real-HOME cache file contained `abcd`; during `m9_render` it was rewritten in place to `wxyz`, the same four-byte size, and its original mtime was restored. The strengthened oracle exited 1 solely on that new row. This is an actual unreported outside-root write, not a hypothetical metadata format concern.

The same snapshot boundary also treats `find` permission-denied/operation-not-permitted and `stat` vanished entries as acceptable records and allows `find` status 1 / `xargs` status through 123. A stable `DENIED` record therefore permits a recursively unreadable subtree to count as a complete snapshot, even though changes below it cannot be observed. Existing F20 covers only a different, unrecognized stat-error form; it does not close this branch. Ambiguous path delimiters and unrecordable names likewise are not refused by this custom line format.

Exact fix-forward minimum:

1. Replace or strengthen the real-HOME recorder so an in-place content rewrite is observable. At minimum, record and compare a SHA-256 for every regular file plus enough lstat identity to bind type, mode, uid/gid, link count, size, nanosecond mtime/ctime, inode and symlink target; exclude only the exact run-root subtree.
2. Fail the before snapshot closed before any workload, and make the after snapshot INCONCLUSIVE, for every traversal/stat/read failure or unrecordable/ambiguous name. A permission-denied, vanished, truncated, malformed, duplicate or incomplete record must never be treated as a complete snapshot or as a stable delta-free path.
3. Extend the registered gate oracle with the demonstrated same-size/restored-mtime rewrite and with an unreadable subtree on both the before and after sides. Add causal mutants dropping content/ctime and accepting an incomplete walk. Preserve all R/J, six real-path and 29 existing gate rows, both final-red oracle bodies, registration, exact-commit provenance, evidence and retention contracts.

The already disclosed early-exit cleanup defect remains an independent Mac-run blocker: the top-level EXIT trap still calls shared `mac_remove_run_dirs`, whose command ends in `true`, does not prove absence and can erase a root without first copying evidence. The separately reviewed `0632602f` cleanup minimum must land before any MS1c run request even after this HOME-audit block is repaired. The real-Mac acceptance must also demonstrate the shared `/var/folders` helper on macOS without an unreported parent/T/C mutation. No push-safe designation or Mac release follows from this review.

The submitted oracle always makes both tools available, so merely adding the nineteen flags satisfies it while retaining this unsafe fallback.

3. Also kill a mutant that ignores or reorders the strict preference inside `detect_runner`.

**BLOCKED; the unregistered red accepts a non-isolating argv.** Exact `d82d12dcb2a1ccdbbd79acc2deae052e65deb51a` honestly adds only `tests/mac/mac_ssh_config_none_selftest.sh`, leaves `build.zig` untouched, passes `git diff --check` and `bash -n`, and runs only fake ssh/scp transports inside a private user/network namespace. The parser collects every `-F` pair anywhere in argv, including after the remote command or scp operands. The x86_64 evidence archive is `77db4bfc9c11163eea5cd4f40af886f93931e53ab5295911ae6876ec479c377f` (317,342,208 bytes); the arm64 archive is `bada550adcd03a1f5a3aba912974855e6a2e64725162b3f54417bd708f494009` (87,787,008 bytes).

The universal-build and packaging hypothesis passed on both machines: both builds passed; both legs used the same DMG `0310b934861e7591d0f0535c3e66befa8fad3acee80b254d470f0f9b605969b9`; attach, copy and detach returned zero; both bundled Mach-O files were fat; strict codesign returned zero; and each requested `otool -arch` slice returned zero with exactly one shim reference. Four harness findings remain, with these exact owners and red-to-green minima:

1. **CodexOtter — `/var/folders` protected-marker producer/consumer mismatch.** This is a shared-helper defect, not a new protected-path exception or an outside-root delta. In `mac_varfolders_audit.sh`, `entry()` obtains lstat metadata and then calls `digest()` in the same try. An approved EACCES/EPERM from `digest()` records `SKIPPED-PROTECTED` and returns before appending the ordinary lstat row. The verifier correctly requires the marker's key to exist in the ordinary row map, so both otherwise complete Intel snapshots are rejected as `malformed protected-skip record`. Red: exercise the real snapshot path on BEFORE and AFTER with lstat succeeding but file open returning EACCES/EPERM for an exact listed path; require one retained ordinary row with SHA `-`, one exact side-tagged marker, one correct END count and an accepted diff. Restore-the-early-return, marker-without-row, wrong-side/reason and transition mutants must be red. Green: split lstat from digest failure handling, retain the captured metadata row with SHA `-`, keep the marker supplementary, and leave every unlisted path or other errno incomplete. Preserve R/V/PC and transition suites and register the new causal row.

2. **CodexHeron — HOME evidence must encode arbitrary filename bytes losslessly.** Intel's HOME files contain no ERROR, but both contain the real entry `zb-bluebeaver/py312/expanded/Python_Applications.pkg/Payload/Python 3.12/Icon\r`. The literal carriage return is written into the tab/newline record format; Python's universal-newline text reader turns it into a record break, so the row count no longer matches `END` and `HOME_DIFF_PY` reports the before snapshot incomplete. Arm64 independently found a readable name containing U+0002 and a tab under `group.com.apple.chronod/.../icons`; the writer intentionally rejects it and stops before workload. These are one serialization defect. **Ruling: change the encoding; no owner policy decision is needed and the name must not be skipped or added to the protected list.** Use one canonical ASCII, reversible encoding of `os.fsencode()` bytes for every relative path and symlink target (for example padded URL-safe Base64 with a fixed prefix), sort by the original bytes, and decode before exact protected-list matching. Red must round-trip CR, LF, tab, U+0002, non-UTF-8 and escape-looking/backslash names without collision or record splitting; identical snapshots are CLEAN and a changed encoded name is a complete HOME-DELTA. Green must update both writer and reader while preserving the exact 98-path permission list, marker transitions, lstat/digest evidence and fail-closed handling of genuine read errors.

3. **CodexHeron — exec-arch socket placement.** The Intel socket path printed in retained evidence is 135 bytes (`.../run-env/tmp/zb-exec-arch-8qdvb4qt/automation.sock`), beyond macOS's bounded Unix-domain pathname. Its failure to appear is therefore a harness placement defect, not proof that the packaged browser lacks the automation API. Red: drive the real probe with a long private TMPDIR/run root and require an explicit short socket path wholly inside the same run root, with socket and token paths preflighted below the macOS bound; restoring `tempfile.mkdtemp()` beneath the long TMPDIR must fail. Green: let the gate give the probe a short directory such as `$RD/s` and a short socket basename, without writing outside the run root. Preserve deletion, token and stale-output checks.

4. **CodexHeron — SIGTERM-only teardown must fail closed on EPERM.** After the socket timeout, `stop_browser_term_only()` let `os.killpg(proc.pid, SIGTERM)` raise `PermissionError`; that traceback masked normal teardown, emitted no LEAK identity and allowed the outer gate to remove the run root. Red: make `killpg` raise EPERM with the browser/group still present and require no exception, exactly one bounded LEAK record with pid/pgid/start/command, one `FAIL<TAB>mac-teardown-leak`, no SIGKILL, a nonzero row and retained/held-host handling. A combined exec-arch case must preserve the original socket diagnostic as well as the teardown failure. Also record `proc.poll()`, pid and actual pgid before signaling; if pgid is not the spawned pid, signal no other group and report/retain it. Green: catch EPERM as an unconfirmed live group and take the existing leak path, so the gate stops that host and retains evidence/root. The subsequent Mac run, if separately approved later, must expose enough bounded process evidence to classify any remaining automation startup failure.

Cleanup returned zero and no gate-owned root remains on either Mac. That confirms filesystem cleanup only. Because the Intel teardown exception produced neither a LEAK identity nor proof that the browser/group exited, **the Intel host is HELD/UNKNOWN, not FREE**, until the owner/orchestrator arranges a human operational process census and any necessary TERM-only cleanup. That recovery is not an MS1c retry. Arm64 launched no browser and its reviewed cleanup completed, so this result adds no corresponding arm64 process hold. No Mac contact was made during this review.

## 2026-10-24 — MS1c HOME codec RED `f3fc78de`

**BLOCKED narrowly: it is red for the intended production reason, but the submitted oracle is not yet satisfiable by the required complete green and does not bind byte ordering.** Exact `f3fc78deb4439cd594987900214759f589bdcd73` adds only executable `tests/mac/ms1c_home_codec_red.sh`, blob SHA-256 `87d73a231d4136ef6c593fd9f4430a31d9d6b47917f9826f6e69b059678b524b`, passes `bash -n` and `git diff --check`, and is absent from `build.zig`. A clean exact archive exits 1 with seven causal codec failures: current production returns incomplete snapshots, emits non-ASCII/raw control-bearing records, and the differ refuses them. There is no missing import or unrelated setup failure.

The intended boundary is mostly sound: the rows require prefixed padded URL-safe Base64, canonical round trips over `os.fsencode()` bytes, no collisions with escape-looking names, exact handling of CR/LF/tab/U+0002/non-UTF-8 filenames, encoded ROOT and protected-marker paths, encoded arbitrary symlink targets, retained ordinary metadata beside the protected marker, exact END counts, a CLEAN identical pair and two encoded rename deltas. Two concrete blockers and two completion gaps remain. First, setup creates `Library/` and `Library/Preferences/` for the protected file. The real snapshotter records every directory, but `wanted_paths` contains only `.`, the protected leaf, the flat names and links.
